# Open Threads

Deferred items and open questions that don't belong to any single document yet — parked here so they're not lost, and revisited at the right stage.

This is a **live tracker**, like the checklists in `Start Here`: it describes work that is still open, and each item is deleted as it closes.

## How this file stays alive

Every item ends with a **Due** line, in one of three shapes: a stage (`at Architecture`), an event (`when the Frames topic's findings land`), or a condition that may never fire (`only if auditor blindness proves leaky`). The line is there so that *what is due now* can be answered by scanning, rather than by reading forty paragraphs and judging each. Anything added here without one is not parked, it is lost.

Whatever has fired is lifted into **Due now**, at the top. Opening this file should answer the question in seconds.

The read happens at every **close** — a research topic closing, an audit round closing, a stage closing. Those are the moments when something has just landed that might fire a trigger, and each already has a runner doing its paperwork. That runner moves what fired into Due now and **offers it to the Creator as work**, not as a note. It used to be read at the start of a stage instead, which was far too coarse: Blender Research is one stage and twelve topics, so a stage-start read fired once for the whole of it, and the first topic to close went by without anyone noticing what it had made due.

This still rests on an agent finishing the last step of its brief. That is a smaller thing to hold than remembering an item exists, and if it proves unreliable the stronger fix is the same one parked below for Rule 7's record.

**Two standing rules about Due lines, both learned the expensive way. Added 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close.**

**Nothing is ever added to this file with a Due naming further Blender research or a further rented machine for answering a question.** The Creator ruled both out on 2026-08-31. A Due naming an event that will not happen is not a park, it is a silence dressed as a plan. **An item that would have wanted one is written as permanently unresolved instead, with what breaks written down** — so the next reader works around a known hole rather than waiting for work that is not coming. *(Build may rent a machine to run the app on, ruled the same day. That is a different act and it revives nothing here.)*

**A close that services work writes it into this file on the day.** Recording it in the documents alone is not enough, and this project has measured the cost: a seam close ran across nineteen architecture documents on 2026-08-22 and told this file nothing, so a dozen items went on reading as owed for eleven days and a runner filed a whole work list against work already done. **The documents were the truth and this file was the record, and only the record was read.**

Three readers followed all of them again into the repaired research. **About 1,450 sites read: forty sentences broken, forty-seven narrowed, two pointers to move, five reaching past the door.** They changed nothing, because several are decisions rather than corrections. - **A rented machine can be left running a job that never ends, and this one was wrong before the repairs rather than because of them.** *(Raised 2026-08-31.)* `Preparation/Specs/Server Setup.md:169` says a render missing an add-on does not stop, it produces the wrong picture — so what the user weighs is a bad render rather than a failure. **Add-ons 7** records the other half: where the engine is named at launch and its add-on is absent, no image is written, three versions end at 1, and **Blender 4.5 does not end at all** — on a rented Linux machine it never exits on its own. `Preparation/Architecture/Sources/Render Settings.sources.md:29` records that Smoozy names the engine on the launch line. **The machine bills until something else stops it.**

- **A mistyped device argument renders the whole job on the processor and ends at success.** *(Raised 2026-08-31.)* After a bare double dash the launch line belongs to Cycles, and an argument Cycles does not know is swallowed without a word. `Preparation/Architecture/Rendering.md:94` puts the device argument exactly there, and that document's name check covers a different kind of instruction. **That is the quiet wrong-device render Row 9 exists to prevent**, arriving by the one route nothing checks.

  ~~**Due:** now, at Architecture.~~ **DONE 2026-09-01, the eighteenth sitting**, at `Rendering` **L24a** — with L8's window given its third edge and the argument's spelling made a fixed constant of the contract at one declaration point, because nothing inside the launch material can ever catch this and no read-back exists for a line Blender never parsed.

- **A setup fault can fire off a stopped run's numbers and restart a paid machine.** *(Raised 2026-08-31.)* `Preparation/Architecture/Server Setup.md:599` reads the line naming what did the work and, where cards were asked for and the processor is named, declares a setup fault and offers to re-run the ready action. It rests on a stopped render never printing that line. Two versions do print it after a stop, **and one version's own fix list calls that block fake with wrong numbers.** Eight places carry the old sentence and six more are in the specs.

- **At every picture size Smoozy renders at, a finished frame prints the mark that means stopped short.** *(Raised 2026-08-31.)* `Preparation/Architecture/Queue & History.md:294` reads the trailing zero as that mark and as nothing else. It holds only below one tile's worth of picture, and above it a run that ended at success with its picture written prints the same line. **Nothing printed separates the two at a working size.**

  ~~**Due:** now, at Architecture, with the specs behind it.~~ **DONE — the architecture half on 2026-08-31 at `Queue & History` R12, the specs behind it on 2026-09-01**, where the rule is now bounded to pictures small enough not to be cut into tiles and the spec also carries the sharper half: **a full count is not evidence a picture exists**, one run having printed the full count, then `Finished`, then `Cancelled`, ended at a failure and left no file.

- **In Blender 4.5 the redirect can put a job's file outside the job's folder.** *(Raised 2026-08-31.)* Where the node gathers its outputs into one file, the folder written into it starts the **file name** instead of naming a folder, so without a trailing separator the file lands beside the folder. That is the harm the redirect exists to prevent, and the name the recipe predicts is then never written — so the already-there test and the completeness statement both report the frame absent.

  ~~**Due:** now, at Architecture.~~ **DONE 2026-09-01, the eighteenth sitting**, at `Job files and outputs` **D8** — the separator is written unconditionally and in every version, because branching it on the node's gathering mode means reading a per-node setting to decide.

**THE MECHANISM SWEEP OF 2026-08-30 RAN AFTER THAT CLOSE AND FOUND WHAT A CLOSED STAGE STILL HAD WRONG.** Seventeen agents read the fifteen comparisons grouped by the part of Blender each question touches — an axis nobody had ever used — and were allowed to open the findings for the conditions a test ran under, which the morning's sweep was forbidden. Every one found something, and every find was a sentence five earlier passes had read and let stand. Around forty were confirmed after five agents tried to kill them. All fifteen comparisons and all ten joined answers are repaired. What follows is what it raised and nobody has picked up.

- **The two things a builder must not carry out of this stage as it stands, and both are already repaired in the research — but nothing downstream has been read against them.** *(Raised 2026-08-30 by the mechanism sweep.)*

  **A frame that finished can print the same mark as a frame that was cut short.** Above one tile's worth of picture — 2048 by 2048, which a 4K frame exceeds — a frame that finished and wrote its file ends on the same zero sample count that means *cut short* below that size. Three joined answers carried the rule with no bound. And separately, a cancelled frame can print a full count and the word *Finished* and leave no file at all. **Nothing a render prints means a frame reached disk except the line naming the file it saved.**

  **Every counting, naming and skipping rule in this stage was measured on a scene writing one file per frame.** A scene with two views writes two, and never writes the base name at all. **Smoozy decides a job finished by counting files.**

  ~~**Due:** Architecture, alongside the fourteen citations the morning's close already left there. Neither is a research question any more; both are things a document downstream may be leaning on wrongly.~~ **BOTH DONE; marked 2026-09-02.** **The finished-frame mark closed 2026-08-31** at `Queue & History` **R12**, bounded to pictures small enough not to be cut into tiles, with the sharper half carried too — a full count is not evidence a picture exists. **The one-file-per-frame measurement basis closed 2026-09-01** at `Job files and outputs` **I9a**: *"Views are not view layers, they do multiply the files, and they change the names — the condition every counting rule in this document was measured under, and it was never stated"*, with §14 items 24 and 25 carrying the residue.

- **A misspelled argument imposed on a render is swallowed in silence and the job renders at success on the wrong device.** *(Raised 2026-08-30 by the mechanism sweep.)* An argument Blender does not know fails the run only to the left of the bare double dash. Every Cycles argument has to sit to the right of it, and there an unknown name is taken without a word. **This is exactly what Smoozy does** — impose a device on a user's file from outside without editing it — and the failure is a full-price render of the wrong thing at an ending that says success.

  ~~**Due:** Architecture, wherever the launch line is composed.~~ **DONE 2026-09-01 at `Rendering` L24a; marked 2026-09-02 as a duplicate.** This file already recorded the same close higher up, under the repairs read downstream on 2026-08-31, and this second copy was never struck. The argument's spelling is a fixed constant of the contract at one declaration point, because nothing inside the launch material can catch it and no read-back exists for a line Blender never parsed.

A freshly rented machine, asked once, before anything else, reports none.

  ~~**Due:** Architecture, at server setup, where a rented machine is asked what it has.~~ **DONE 2026-08-22 at `Rendering` L21a; marked 2026-09-02.** Observed on two rented multi-card boxes in both versions: `compute_device_type` arrives as `NONE` from factory startup **while every device in the list arrives with `use = True`**, and the clause states that *"nothing may infer from the device list that a render will use the cards."* **L23 additionally handles a hardware reading that returns no card at all.** The machine-side hardware reading is `Servers` §15 and holds no Blender in it (H16), so the cold-machine case this entry names is answered on both sides.

- **Four cards are faster than one, and slower than one, and no sentence in this stage named the condition that decides which.** *(Raised 2026-08-30 by the mechanism sweep, now repaired in the research.)* On a scene using a tenth of a card, four cards ran 3.6 times faster. On the same machine, the same day, the same version, with a scene that overflows one card, four ran 3.8 times slower. **That is the sentence a farm prices a rental with**, and anything in this project that reasons about what a bigger machine is worth should be read against it.

  **Due:** the Creator, or Architecture, whenever what a rental is worth is decided.

Only one of the questions behind it states that ceiling itself. It is now written at the top of the joined answer that owns it.

- **Two comparisons say opposite things about what a stopped render leaves behind for the frame it was working on, and that decides whether resuming a stopped job silently loses a frame.** *(Raised 2026-08-30 by the cross-sweep's absolutes lens, candidate 2, at the stage close. Neither side was chosen and neither could be.)*

  `Writing` question 11 says a render stopped part-way **leaves the empty placeholder file for the frame in hand behind, at zero bytes**, alongside the whole frames already done — both machines, all four versions. `Frames` question 10 says an interruption **leaves whole frames only**, in every version on both machines, and that with placeholders on **no placeholder was left behind either**, which it records as disproving exactly the inference `Writing` states. **Same arrangement, same kind of stop, opposite contents of the output folder.**

  **Why it matters more than it looks, in the sweep's own finding.** `Frames` question 14 settles that **a placeholder left behind by a killed render blocks its frame for good** — a later render skips it and leaves it at zero bytes, unopenable, so a render that dies leaves a hole no later render fills. **So one of the two readings makes an automatic re-run safe and the other makes it lossy**, and Smoozy's whole re-run promise sits on top of it.

  **Neither side's evidence could have come out the other way**, which is why reading harder will not settle it: `Writing` records the placeholder appearing within about half a second of a frame starting, so a stop landing anywhere inside a frame lands while one exists. **It needs a run, not a reading**, and the sweep says so.

  ~~**Due: now.** It is a Blender Research question with no number yet, and it is the sharpest thing the cross-sweep raised.~~ **PERMANENTLY UNRESOLVED, marked 2026-09-02 at the Architecture stage close. This Due can never fire.** The Creator ruled on 2026-08-31 that there will be **no further Blender research and no further rented machine for answering a question**. *(Build may rent a machine to run the app on, ruled the same day — a different act, and it does not revive this.)* **This item's own text says why no reading can rescue it:** *"It needs a run, not a reading, and the sweep says so"*, and neither side's evidence could have come out the other way. **What breaks, written down rather than left as a silence:** `Preparation/Architecture/Queue & History.md`'s re-run reckoning and `Job files and outputs` **C33** both rest on which of the two readings is true. **Under one reading an automatic re-run is safe; under the other it silently loses a frame**, because a leftover placeholder blocks its frame for good and a later render skips it at zero bytes. **So nothing that restarts a stopped job may trust the file count**, and whoever builds the re-run treats a file's presence as evidence of nothing except that a file is present. It is left here in full so what it costs stays visible. **Its twin, `Frames 16`, carries the same silence from the other end.**

- **The heaviest question three architecture documents record as owed is answered, and it was answered four days before anybody noticed.** *(This is the entry below, fired 2026-08-30 by the downstream citation pass.)*

  **ANSWERED at *Writing 13*, folded into the comparison on 2026-08-26 — sixteen hand tests, every one in all four versions, on the Creator's Mac and on a rented Linux machine.** **Order decides what the script sees.** With the file first, then the setting, then the script, the script reads the value handed in; with the script written before the setting, it reads the file's own path; with the setting written before the file, the file load wipes it. The long form of the argument behaves identically to the short one. **The output-path value lands in exactly one place — the active scene's own render output path — and nowhere else**, found by searching every data-block rather than by guessing a name. **And `Job files and outputs` D10's read-back design was tested end to end**: in the right arrangement it matched, the render ran, one frame was written and the launch ended at 0; in the wrong arrangement nothing rendered and the launch ended at 1.

  **Three cautions travel with it, all the question's own.** The thread count does **not** obey the order it was written in. The `--cycles-device` value lands nowhere a script can read. And the output-path argument reaches the **active scene alone**, with the question's own *not established* on whether that turns on which scene is active — **which is a live hazard for a file holding more than one scene.**

  ~~**Due: now, at Architecture.** Four unresolved items and one send-back can be closed — `Job files and outputs` §14 item 19, `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 11, `Rendering` §9 item 1, that document's D10, and the register entry — and **closing them is a decision, which is why the citation pass rightly closed none.**~~ **DONE 2026-08-31; marked 2026-09-02.** **All three architecture items are closed** — `Rendering` §9 item 1 (*"ANSWERED … and the answer is the good one"*), `Job files and outputs` §14 item 19, and `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 11 — and D10's read-back stands supported rather than assumed. **The residue is item 19a**, which scene an imposed output path reaches on a file holding more than one; it is stated and bounded rather than owed. **What is left is the Question register's send-back alone, and that file is Blender Research's** — routed there, not Architecture's to touch. *(The original entry follows, unchanged.)*

- **Whether a value delivered by a command-line argument is in force by the time a script handed in at the same launch runs.** The twin of the entry above, and the opposite shape. `Rendering` L10 delivers the regular output path by its own command-line argument and has the launch material **read the value back**, failing the attempt where it does not match the destination map's pair — which is the positive check standing between the app and a render that writes where nobody asked. **If the argument is not yet in force when the material runs, the read-back sees the file's own recorded path, mismatches, and every attempt fails before the render instruction — no job renders anywhere.** Nothing in the register asks it: *Settings 1* and *What a render demands 3* cover which values have arguments, *Writing 1* covers imposing an output path, and *Settings 16* and *Dependencies 17* cover the window an argument must sit in — none asks about the **order** in which an argument's value and a handed-in script take effect inside that window. **This is a Blender behaviour and Rule 2 forbids settling it here.** What holds meanwhile is that the failure is safe and total rather than silent, which is why three architecture documents carried it without escalating — `Job files and outputs` §14 item 19, `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 11 and `Rendering` §9 item 1. It is written here because three unresolved lists are the one place Method says a reader will not look. Recorded in full in `Preparation/Architecture/Facts.md` under the facts that leave the stage. **When:** at Blender Research, in the **Settings** or **Reports** topic. *(Raised by `Rendering`'s Round 1, 2026-08-19.)*

  ~~**Due:** at Blender Research, whenever that stage next runs.~~ **Spent — see the paragraphs above this entry. *Writing 13* answered it and the Due line above is the live one.**

  **What is now known around it, and it makes the gap sharper rather than smaller.** *Reports 12* establishes that a Python script that **fails** leaves the run at 0 with the render going ahead. *Writing 13* establishes what a script sees of a command-line setting when it does run. *Reports 14* establishes that **a script's own printed lines do not reach a pipe or a file until the run ends** — so the `Permission denied` this item is built on would not reach a reader watching a pipe until the wrong render had already finished. **Three answered questions now surround the unasked one**, and every one of them makes the silent-wrong-render case easier to hit rather than harder.

  **1. The power cut, and it is not a rental you are being asked for.** What you declined was pulling the plug on your own Mac. **A guest machine killed at the hypervisor was never put to you** — it never touches the Mac's own power. Four agents independently confirmed the Mac has **no hypervisor and no container runtime installed at all**, and that single absence is what blocks every power-cut, ext4, cron and case-folding question the round turned up — not money, and not a rented machine. Installing free virtualisation software is your act. **Two honest caveats:** the guest would be arm64 while every rental is x86_64, which nothing in the filesystem or the service manager cares about; and a virtual machine still does not remove a physical drive's write cache, which is the half Fact 44's own reasoning is built on. So it closes one half and leaves the other open. **One more thing was added to that machine's list on 2026-08-20**, and it is a single command: create a case-folding directory, make a file with mixed capitals, list the directory, and see which spelling comes back. It settles an internal contradiction in `Dependency Upload & Remap` — one clause there says the walk gets the stored name on a folding disk and another says there is no route to it — and it answers, in the same breath, whether the folding option behaves as its documentation says. **Due:** yours.

  **8a. Added 2026-08-21, and it closes a promise rather than opening one.** Row 293 promises that two keys Smoozy knows can never share a fingerprint. Its world-side half — that two distinct keys can never share one at all — has been carried as *stated by nothing cited* in four places, and **the question turns out to have been asked in the wrong form**. The printed fingerprint's hash is **selectable**, and the answer flips between the two forms: **in MD5 two keys sharing one is a documented, cheap construction** — RFC 6151, *"can find MD5 collision in about one minute on a standard notebook PC"* — and **in SHA-256 nobody has ever produced one.** The rule that follows needed nobody's ruling and is now written into `Keys` F8 and `Security and licence` §6: **pin the hash to SHA-256 explicitly and never treat an MD5 fingerprint as an identity.** **What is left for you is one sentence's worth of judgment.** No first-party source says a SHA-256 collision does not exist, because an absence of published collisions is not a citable statement — so the clause can either stay open for ever on a citation that will never come, or close on the same footing this project already accepts elsewhere: **it carries no equivalent worry about somebody guessing a private key**, which rests on exactly the same kind of reasoning. **Nothing is blocked either way**; every mechanism works identically under both answers. ~~**Due:** yours.~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close, on the footing the project already accepts.** **The clause does not stay open for ever on a citation that can never come.** No first-party source will ever say a SHA-256 collision does not exist, because an absence of published collisions is not a citable statement — so *waiting for the source* is waiting for something with no arrival date. **This project already carries no equivalent worry about somebody guessing a private key**, which rests on exactly the same kind of reasoning, and holding this one open while that one is closed is an inconsistency rather than a caution. **What was never in doubt and is already written:** `Keys` **F8** and `Security and licence` §6 pin the fingerprint hash to SHA-256 explicitly and forbid treating an MD5 fingerprint as an identity, on RFC 6151's documented one-minute MD5 construction. **That is the rule that mattered, it needed nobody's ruling, and it stands unchanged.** Row 293's promise is closed on the SHA-256 half.

  **8. The proposed project-wide rule, carried from the sitting before.** A parked question, or any claim that something cannot be done, must also say what a knowledgeable person would expect the answer to be. It would have caught most of one fault shape and all of another. **It changes how the whole project works and is not enacted.** ~~**Due:** yours.~~ **DECLINED 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close, and recorded with its reason so nobody re-opens it.** **The reason is blindness.** It is this project's loudest standing rule: `CLAUDE.md` opens by telling Blender researchers to read nothing here, the Creator's own recollections are quarantined by name in three sources files, and the Method records that *"a question naming a feature dies the day it is renamed."* **A rule requiring every parked question to state its expected answer writes the hypothesis down beside the question**, in files researchers are forbidden to read — survivable in itself, but it habituates the whole project to writing hypotheses beside open questions, which is the exact failure the quarantine exists to prevent. **And most of its value is already had**: the standing rule that a parked question says **which publication was looked at** does the same work — it says what was checked, without saying what anybody expects to find. **What is given up, stated rather than glossed:** the item's own credit stands, that it would have caught most of one fault shape and all of another, and that gain is being declined for the discipline. **Not enacted project-wide, not enacted for Architecture alone, and not enacted in the narrower shape that would apply it only to claims that something cannot be done** — a rule with an exception is a rule people forget which side they are on.

  **9. Added 2026-08-21, and it is a cost question rather than a correction.** To find out which key types a machine's key generator will accept, `Keys` T2 generates a throwaway key of each of the three supported types and deletes it at once. **A documented instant alternative exists** — `ssh -Q key-plain`, which writes nothing and returns immediately — **but it answers a narrower question**, established rather than guessed: it names algorithms rather than the values `-t` accepts and splits one accepted type into three curves, so Smoozy would need a mapping rule the project would then have to source; it includes the two authenticator-hosted types T3 deliberately excludes; and it asks the wrong program, so it cannot do the second job T2's probe does, which is detecting that the key generator is missing — the live risk on exactly one system, a customised Windows image, where the client and the generator are separate components. **The cost of keeping T2 was measured rather than guessed:** 0.03 s, 0.03 s and 0.51 s, so about six-tenths of a second once per application run, and three private key files written and deleted in Smoozy's own temporary area — not the key location, and nothing the user sees. **The choice is yours: pay it to keep one probe that also catches a missing generator, or split the generator probe from the type probe.** T2 is not wrong and nothing breaks either way. *(One route was checked and rejected on the project's own rule: the generator prints its accepted list in its usage text, and reading that is parsing an undocumented diagnostic string, which `Security and licence` §13 rule 5 forbids.)* ~~**Due:** yours.~~ **RULED 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close: T2 stays exactly as it is, and the reason is recorded in `Keys`.** **The case is measured rather than argued.** The cost is 0.03 s, 0.03 s and 0.51 s — about six-tenths of a second once per application run — and three private key files written and deleted in Smoozy's own temporary area, **not the key location, and nothing the user ever sees.** **The instant alternative answers a narrower question**, established rather than guessed: `ssh -Q key-plain` names algorithms rather than the values `-t` accepts, splits one accepted type into three curves so Smoozy would need a mapping rule the project would then have to source, includes the two authenticator-hosted types T3 deliberately excludes, and **asks the wrong program** — so it cannot do the second job T2's probe does, which is detecting that the key generator is missing. **That risk is live on exactly one system**, a customised Windows image where the client and the generator are separate components. **So the probe is not paying six-tenths of a second for one answer; it is paying it for two, one of which nothing else can give.** Nothing breaks either way, which is why this was the Creator's to weigh and why the stage is content to close it.

  **11. A rental left running runs until somebody destroys it by hand, and nothing caps it. Filed 2026-08-22, and it was required to be raised on 2026-08-20 when the mitigation was struck.** The document half landed and the filing never happened, so this is the round's largest money item and it has been living in one batch record. **What is true:** the provider publishes no way to cap a rental's life — `duration` is not a parameter of the create call and the end date is documented as locked at rental — and a watchdog inside the container cannot end it either, because the kernel forbids a process from killing its own namespace's init. So the only protection that exists is the habit of destroying a rental the moment it has answered and checking the account empty afterwards. **What that costs you:** an agent that crashes, a chat that is closed, or a machine forgotten between sittings bills until a human notices. ~~**Due:** yours — whether that habit is enough, or whether something outside the machine should be watching the account.~~ **ANSWERED 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close, and it splits into a product half and a habit half.**

  **The product half — what Smoozy does about it.** **Smoozy states plainly, at the point of renting, that the machine runs and bills until it is destroyed by hand**, and **shows how long it has been running wherever the machine is shown.** That is the whole answer: the user is told the shape of the exposure at the moment they take it on, and is given the number that makes it real afterwards. **No automatic destruction is built, deliberately** — a rescue that destroys a machine can destroy work, and a wrong judgement about idleness ends a paying render or a job somebody is waiting on. **A telling is recoverable and a destruction is not.** The architecture half of this is being written into `Rented-server runtime` by the implementer that holds it; this file records the ruling, not the mechanism. **DONE, marked 2026-10-16 by owed piece 9 of Build step 10c, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Open Threads' Due now and the board's owed lines — what is still owed, in pieces.md`.** The product half is built: `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/servers.rs` lines 547 and 548 (`RUNS_AND_BILLS`, `RUNNING_FOR`).

  **The habit half — this project's own rentals.** **The habit stands as it is: destroy the moment the machine has answered, then check the account empty.** **Nothing outside the machine is built to watch it**, because the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31 means **no further machine is rented to answer a question at all**, so the remaining exposure is Build's rentals only — a much smaller thing than this item was written against. **The two mechanical negatives stand and neither is going to change:** the provider publishes no way to cap a rental's life — `duration` is not a parameter of the create call and the end date is documented as locked at rental — and **a watchdog inside the container cannot end it either**, because the kernel forbids a process from killing its own namespace's init. **What the habit does not catch, written down rather than glossed:** an agent that crashes, a chat that is closed, or a machine forgotten between sittings bills until a human notices — and this project's own record shows the failure is real, the standing-machine item at the top of this file having read as live for three days after the machine was destroyed, with the destruction found by a rewrite rather than by a check. **Two things this stage left on providers' consoles are the residue of exactly that**, and they are the item added at the end of this section.

- **~~Three things this stage left outside the repository, and no agent can close any of them.~~ ALL THREE CLOSED 2026-09-02, by the Creator, the day they were written here.** *(Added here 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close, from `Audits/Architecture/Routed elsewhere.md`. They were on the audit board and in no tracker, and the audit folder is not where anybody looks after a stage closes.)* **Each entry below said it closes when the Creator does it and somebody writes it down; this is the writing down, and the entries are struck rather than deleted because how each got there is the part worth keeping.** **What does not close with them is the habit at item 11 above** — the account-empty checks look at instances, volumes and snapshots and never at rules or stored keys, so a rule or a key left behind still survives every test this project has. **Build rents machines, so Build's own check has to look at both.**

  **They want the Creator's own hands, on somebody else's console or on their own computer.** All three are the residue of the habit named in item 11 above — **this project's account-empty checks look at instances, volumes and snapshots, and never at rules or stored keys**, so nothing a later check does could have caught the first two.

  **1. ~~A firewall rule open to the whole internet, on a machine that no longer exists.~~ CLOSED 2026-09-02 — rule 3164286 deleted on the Hyperstack console.** On the **Hyperstack** console: security-group rule **id 3164286, ingress tcp 8443 from 0.0.0.0/0**, added through the provider's API so a Telegram webhook could reach the machine. **The machine is destroyed and the rule is not.** It closes when the Creator deletes rule 3164286 and somebody writes down that it is gone.

  **2. ~~A throwaway SSH key left on a second provider's account.~~ CLOSED 2026-09-02 — `smoozy-arch-batch` removed from the Vast dashboard.** On the **Vast** dashboard: `smoozy-arch-batch`, added during a machine batch. **The account key lacks the route to delete it**, so it wants removing by hand. Its private half never left the scratchpad of the sitting that made it. It closes when the Creator removes it and somebody writes down that it is gone.

  **3. ~~A file an agent left in the Creator's own home folder and cannot remove.~~ CLOSED 2026-09-02 — the file is deleted.** `~/Library/Application Support/com.apple.TCC/smzprobe`, 1,048,576 bytes, written 2026-08-18 by a reading agent that overstepped. **Four removal attempts were refused** and the record says it was verified still there. Outside the repository like the other two, though not on a provider's console.

  ~~**Due:** the Creator, on the providers' own consoles and on their own Mac.~~ **DONE 2026-09-02, all three.** **No agent could close any of the three**, and none of them blocked anything — the first was a security exposure on a dead machine, the second a stored credential the project no longer uses, the third a stray file. **They were written here so they would stay visible after the audit folder stopped being read, and they were closed the same day** — which is the one clean demonstration this file has that being written in the right place is what gets a thing done.

Noted 2026-08-30.** That equipment section was rewritten twice at the Blender Research close the next day — four faults repaired in it, and four machines added — **and no Architecture rental before the multi-provider batch went in with them.** So the sentence naming a home nobody uses is still there, and the section it names has just been through the most thorough correction it has ever had.

- **`Download` was written owing two seams; the first was a spec and a design document contradicting each other and is now ruled, the second is still open.** *(From the document's filing, 2026-08-20. Both recorded rather than repaired; no closed document was edited.)*

  **The Download spec and the Transfers design disagree about whether a single transfer can be stopped by hand.** [Download](../Specs/Download.md) says that where a user wants one transfer gone immediately they *"can stop it by hand, like any other transfer"* — Creator-stated, 2026-07-22, in the paragraph settling that switching auto-download off lets the transfers already under way finish. [Transfers](../Design/Transfers.md) rules the opposite: *"There is no Stop on a single file"*, because stopping one file of three hundred is not a thing the user asked for and a control that looked like it would be read as one — Creator-stated, 2026-08-13. **The architecture supports both** (`Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` 8.5 M12: Stop acts on the whole request, and the mechanism beneath can stop any single transfer), so nothing is blocked and nothing is built wrong either way. **What is owed is which control exists**, and it is a Specs-and-Design question rather than an architecture one: the spec's sentence is the user's escape route from a heavy file they no longer want, and the design's reasoning is about a request of three hundred rather than about an arrangement's handful.

  **And `Job files and outputs`' supply paragraph names the inventory but not the attempt record's frame list.** `Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` Section 4 reads that list to get the frame order a later attempt renders in — the order its live judgement keys on — and the attempt records are that document's 3.4. Its *To `Download`* paragraph hands over the inventory, the count, the one folder and the video shape, and does not name the attempt records. **Nothing breaks without it**: where the list cannot be read the live mechanism does not run for that attempt and the catch-up mechanism alone decides, which the document states. **One line of that supply paragraph closes it.**

  **Ruled 2026-08-20: Design complies.** Creator-stated. The spec's per-file stop stands and the Transfers screen owes the control; [Transfers](../Design/Transfers.md) now says so and writes the old ruling up as reversed rather than deleting it, because the risk it named is real and is what the appearance has to solve. **Nothing about the appearance was decided, at the Creator's own instruction**, and `Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` 8.5 M12 and Section 15 item 13 are brought into line — the mechanism did not move, since that clause already said any single transfer can be stopped.

  ~~**Due: when Design next takes the Transfers screen, and not before — that is the Creator's own instruction.**~~ **Due: done, 2026-08-24**, at the `Transfers` mockup seam, round 1 — which is when Design next took that screen.

  **Most of the appearance had already been settled on 2026-08-23**, when the reminder marker under that document's *Left for later* was closed: the control is in the row's own menu and never on the row, it asks nothing, and a file with nothing left to stop has no Stop rather than a greyed one. *(This entry used to say the marker was still standing. It was closed that day and the sentence had gone stale.)*

- **`Render Settings` Round 1 added one more seam, and it sharpened another from a line into a step.** *(From Round 1 closing, 2026-08-20. Both recorded rather than repaired; no closed document was edited. The entry below is the filing-time snapshot and stands as written.)*

  **`Queue & History` N10 does not keep the override-or-automatic distinction on a history entry, and `Render Settings` Y15 needs it.** A page opened from a history entry loads that job's kept setup, which is a payload — and a payload always carries a resolved Blender version, whether the user picked it or the automatic match did. Y15 settles that only the entries that were **the user's own answers** become store entries, the version pick among them **only where the entry records it as an override**; otherwise the automatic match runs afresh for the machine in hand. **N10 does not say the entry keeps that distinction.** **What breaks without it:** a version pick nobody made rides into the store, the automatic match never runs on the new machine, and the page offers a build that machine may not have — worst case a same-numbered build at another path, rendering on a version chosen for a machine that no longer exists. **What is owed:** one clause in `Queue & History` N10. `Render Settings` Section 13 item 23 carries it and Section 12's supply line seconds it.

  **And the third of the four smaller seams below is now a step rather than a line.** `Job files and outputs` M1's field list was recorded as read two ways; what is actually owed reaches further — **`Rendering` P1 gains a validation step**, between steps 4 and 7, at which every name-shaped payload entry is checked against the reading in force and a name not found fails the attempt before the render instruction. **Calling it a line in a field list invites a repair that does not build the step.** Recorded here because the entry below words it the old way.

And the third smaller seam was built as the **step** this entry insisted on rather than as a line in a field list: `Rendering` **P1a**, the validation step, placed between steps 4 and 7 without renumbering anything.
