- **Whether a computer says *there is no such program* the way Smoozy relies on, on Windows.** *(Filed by the Keys round-1 consolidator, 2026-09-03. Checked against this file first and it was not here; the standing Windows batch names ten questions and not this one.)*

  Smoozy tells *the key tool is absent* from *the key tool refused* by one thing: the error a failed spawn reports. **That branch is observed on macOS alone.** If a Windows machine without the OpenSSH capability reports anything else, **every key on that computer is marked broken when nothing is wrong with any of them**, and the one line the spec promises above the list never appears.

  **It splits, and only half of it is parked.** What a spawn of a missing program reports is answerable on the hosted Windows and Linux runners this project already uses, in one small test, with nothing rented — **that half is owed at step 4's runner run and is not parked.** Whether the key tool is on the path a Smoozy launched from a Windows desktop inherits wants a real desktop, and **that half joins the standing Windows batch above.**

  **Due:** the first half at step 4; the second half whenever the standing Windows batch is answered, and never by a guess in the meantime. The narrowing is already written where the branch sits.

  **THE FIRST HALF'S TRIGGER HAS FIRED. Marked 2026-09-03, at the step 4 close, so it is not left reading as future.** Step 4 is the moment this item named. **Whether the runner run was performed is not recorded here, and it does not look as though it was**: on 2026-09-03 no test that spawns a missing program was found anywhere in the workspace, and the branch this rests on is still the one reading a missing-file error from a failed spawn. **If it was not run, it is owed at the next step's runner run**, unchanged and still costing one small test on machines this project already builds on. **The second half is untouched** and stays with the Windows batch.

  **STILL NOT DONE AT STEP 5'S CLOSE. Marked 2026-09-04, and confirmed by opening the code rather than by a claim.** `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/keys/readings.rs` still reads *there is no such program* off a missing-file error from a failed spawn, and both `there_is_a_generator` and `run` still say in place that what a spawn of a missing program reports on Windows and on Linux **is established by nothing this project has read or run**. No test that spawns a missing program was found in the workspace. **So the first half is owed again, at step 6's runner run**, unchanged and still one small test on machines this project already builds on.

  **STILL NOT DONE AT STEP 6'S CLOSE. Marked 2026-09-04, and confirmed by opening the code rather than by a claim.** `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/keys/readings.rs` still reads *there is no such program* off a missing-file error from a failed spawn, and `run` still says in place that what a spawn of a missing program reports on Windows and on Linux **is established by nothing this project has read or run**. No test that spawns a missing program was found in the workspace. **So the first half is owed again, at step 7's runner run** — the third step in a row it has gone past. **The second half is untouched** and stays with the Windows batch.

  **THE TEST IS WRITTEN. 2026-09-05, at step 8's opening, by the build runner sending one agent at it rather than waiting for a fifth close to go past it.** `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/keys/readings.rs` now carries `what_a_spawn_of_a_missing_program_reports`, beside the branch it is about. It spawns a program that certainly does not exist **in the same construction the real call uses**, in two shapes — a bare name, which is Smoozy's own case, and an absolute path to nothing — and prints the error kind, the operating-system error number, the text, and **which of Smoozy's two answers that error takes**, spelled out. It writes to the error stream rather than the output stream, so the reading lands in a runner log with no `--nocapture` and no change to the workflow.

  **It asserts almost nothing on purpose**: that the spawn fails, and on macOS alone that the kind is the one the branch already rests on. **Nothing is asserted about Windows or Linux**, because nothing is known about them, and an assertion there would have been the guess this item exists to prevent.

  **One thing was read and it settles less than it looks.** The library's own page documents that a spawn fails where the program cannot be found — *"the program could not be found (for example, it does not exist, or, when given a bare name, it is not present in the `PATH`)"*, https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/process/struct.Command.html, read 2026-09-05, label **documented**. **That page names no error kind and no operating-system error number for any system.** So the half that decides Smoozy's branch is undocumented and only the run answers it. Microsoft's own published error for a missing image was deliberately not taken as the answer, because the same page says Rust resolves the path itself on Windows rather than handing the name over — so that reading answers a different question.

  **macOS, read at the desk the same day:** kind `NotFound`, operating-system error 2, both shapes.

  ~~**Due:** step 8's hosted runner run, which is the next one.~~ **ANSWERED 2026-09-05, run `33994092102`, green on all three systems. THE FIRST HALF IS CLOSED AND THE BRANCH IS RIGHT.** Both systems report the kind Smoozy's branch reads, and Smoozy takes both as *the tool is absent* — `Keys` G6 answers no generator and `Keys` U6a marks every key not checked, which is the intended outcome and not the feared one. **Nobody's keys are marked broken on a Windows computer without the capability.** The readings, in the log's own words:

  - **Windows, a bare name on no path:** kind `NotFound`, **no operating-system error number at all**, text *"program not found"*.
  - **Windows, an absolute path to nothing:** kind `NotFound`, error 2, text *"The system cannot find the file specified. (os error 2)"*.
  - **Linux, both shapes:** kind `NotFound`, error 2, text *"No such file or directory (os error 2)"*.
  - **macOS, both shapes**, read at the desk the same day: kind `NotFound`, error 2.

  **The Windows bare-name shape is the one worth carrying, and it is exactly the case Smoozy is in.** `THE_TOOL` is a bare name, and on Windows that shape comes back with **no operating-system error number** — because Rust resolves the path itself before spawning rather than handing the name over, which the library's own page says in as many words. **So anything reading the error number rather than the kind would have got nothing on the one system this was filed about.** The branch reads the kind, and that is why it holds.

  **What this cost, in the end: one small test, four step closes late.** It could have been run at step 4.

  **The second half is untouched** and stays with the Windows batch: whether the key tool is on the path a Smoozy launched from a Windows desktop inherits. **That one wants a real desktop and is permanently unresolved.**

The page's *20 assets* against *31* on one file during a walk. *Can't work out the bake folder* is still a guess's words. A quit between a library's publication and its fixing holds nothing at the next run, and a press that finds a library already at its mirror starts no check, so either can still make a job without that library (`Dependency Upload & Remap` A9e); `Rendering` S11's *Submit the remaining scenes* is not read against the hold. **Due: the next build round on the dependency check, or as soon as a user meets one.**

- **What the 5.3 integration left: renders and pages.** *(Parked by the build runner, 2026-11-03.)* A preview JPEG beside an EXR is left out of the page's count; it comes home, so only the figure is short (`use_preview`, observed on 5.3 alone). A loaded setup's other answers stand on the next page, as its range did until now: samples, percentage, engine, device, pins, the ticked scene, a version override, and a failed entry's Simplify, which is at least drawn, against `Rendering` A1c's *never an act carried over from an earlier job* (`Render Settings` Y15b). A script reading its own folder now sees the opening folder. `RuntimeWarning` and `SyntaxWarning` from a file's own scripts land on *Errors*. A server program older than this release holds a strip's path until it follows the release. **Due: the next build round on Render Settings, or as soon as a user meets one.**

- **What the 5.3 integration left: the machine.** *(Parked by the build runner, 2026-11-03.)* Driver faults 2, 5 and 6 in `The driver swap — what landed.md`: the live header keeps the connection's driver reading, recovery past *restart issued* only checks, and `a_swap_may_begin` also asks the CUDA summary. After a swap on an image with NVIDIA's archive, `libnvidia-cfg.so.1` stands at 615's; it did no harm, and a later upgrade of it is not established. `assets/remaps` keeps a small file after a job's records go. A reading of Telegram's role that could not be had stays unread for that connection. **Permanently unresolved, and what breaks:** a server on a driver of 575 to 579 was never rented, so the 580 bar rests on 5.3's Cycles file, and a card there may read as disabled; `Illegal address` met on one card family (RTX 5060 Ti on 595.84) with hand controls too, so that family may fail on 5.3 with Blender's own words. **Due: the next build round on Server Setup, or as soon as a user meets one.**

When 5.3 is released, its manual, API documentation and release notes are copied again into `Blender Sources/` and compared page by page with that copy, and only the questions whose pages moved are asked again; any answer that changes goes into its comparison, and `Preparation/Blender Research/What 5.3 changed.md` is brought into line. The research board, `Audits/Blender Research/Progress.md`, says how. **Due: when Blender 5.3.0 is released.**

Tagged for `Queue & History` E13a and §18 item 27.)* `Queue & History` E13a's telling stands unbuilt in every version. *Dependencies 15* now quotes 5.3's lines, so a reader for 5.3 alone could be written, from text seen on a beta on a Mac whose words may still move. Until it is, a 5.3 entry says nothing about a colour substitution, as the four before it do. 5.4's text has not been read. **Due: after the release re-read in the item above.**

- **Blender installs still waiting when Smoozy closes.** *(Raised by the builder of the Creator's third notes, piece H — several Blenders installed one after another — and parked by the build runner, 2026-10-08.)* When several Blenders are ticked and installed one after another and Smoozy closes before the last has started, the waiting ones are never begun, and nothing names them when Smoozy opens again; only the Blender table shows what is really there. The plans have no words for this, and saying it would mean keeping the waiting list in the local record. **A Design gap, not a fault**: nothing false is said. **Due:** when the Creator meets it, or with the next Design pass on Server Setup. Account: `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's third notes, piece H — what landed.md`.

Parked by the plan writer holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — add-ons parked and a file's scripts run, the plan pass.md`.)* The Creator, word for word: *"You know what? Let's ditch add-ons for now. You can put my own wording wherever the reasons for that go — I feel like this will create more problems than it will solve. Maybe someday when I have all the add-ons that fit the criteria of running at render time myself I will get back to it. But right now — we're not building it."* **What is parked:** `Specs/Server Setup` § *Add-ons*, `Architecture/Server Setup` §6 (A1 to A9), Render Settings' hand-off to Server Setup, and the 38's piece 18 and P6 (the add-ons section and its drawing, taken out). **What stays:** Pre-load still finds a file whose engine this Blender lacks, and Render Settings says so with nothing to install. **What to read when it comes back:** the two web scouts, `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Render engines for Blender — a web scout, 2026-10-08.md` and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Add-ons needed at render time — a web scout, 2026-10-08.md`, and the superseded rulings 1 to 3 and P6's open question in the rulings file. **Due:** when the Creator holds the add-ons that run at render time.

Parked by the build runner.)* `Architecture/File Browser` L22 and P9's acts on several files, and the toolbar's *1 selected*, imagine choosing several rows; the app holds one, with no Shift-click, so Design's Shift-with-arrow was struck on 2026-10-06. **Due:** when the Creator asks for choosing several files, or when a pass finds a user missing it — then one piece: the count words, Shift-click and Shift-arrow, and delete, rename, cut, copy and the right-click menu on several.

  **CLOSED 2026-10-18: the Creator asked for it** (the thirteenth notes, note 6: "Why can't I ctrl/cmd+click to select multiple files in the file browser?"). `Design/File Browser` § *Acting on files* now says how rows are chosen and what each act does with several, and the struck Shift-with-arrow sentence is restored; the drawing has a *3 selected* state (`fb-multi`); piece G of the thirteenth notes builds it. The item is kept as written. *Closed by the plan writer holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the thirteenth notes, the plan pass and the drawing pass.md`.*

- **Three Architecture records still say what `Telegram` said before its first build audit.** *(Found by the implementer of `Telegram` round 1, *the bot and the chat*, 2026-09-14, and confirmed by that round's close check. Parked by the build runner.)* `Preparation/Architecture/Rows/Telegram.md` **Row 160** still quotes Y3's old wording, from before *try it again* was ruled a submission a lock refuses. **Row 50** and `Preparation/Architecture/Facts.md` **Fact 64** still say every pin is cleared at each hand-over, which U2 of that round narrowed. The plan (`Telegram.md`) is right and the code follows the plan; these are records that stopped being true. **Breaks if left:** a reader who starts from a row or fact rather than the plan is misled. **Due:** at the Build stage close, or sooner if either file is next edited for any reason.

- **Telegram publishes a slicker way to make a bot, and it would replace the whole copy-and-paste walkthrough.** *(Found by the architecture runner at the tenth sitting, 2026-08-25. **Parked on the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-25.** A want under Rule 7, not a hole.)*

  **What exists today.** The user is walked through making their own bot by hand: they talk to Telegram's own bot-making bot, pick a name, and copy the token back into Smoozy. It is drawn, it is designed, and the architecture is written for it.

  **What Telegram publishes instead.** A bot Smoozy owns can hand the user a pre-filled bot-making window and collect the new token itself, with no copying.

  **Why it is parked rather than taken.** It is plainly better and it arrived after the walkthrough was drawn, specified and built into the architecture. Taking it now would churn Design and Architecture for something already finished, on the eve of Build. **Due:** the Creator, after Build, if the walkthrough turns out to be the friction it looks like.

- **Whether a Linux that is Ubuntu underneath but calls itself something else should be admitted.** *(Raised by the architecture runner on 2026-08-22. **Ruled on 2026-08-25: the refusal stands**, and it is parked here rather than deleted because the reasoning is worth keeping.)*

  Smoozy prepares Ubuntu and Debian and refuses every other family plainly. A machine like Linux Mint or Pop!_OS is Ubuntu underneath in every way this module touches, and today Smoozy refuses it.

  **The Creator ruled the refusal stands.** It costs one sentence, installs nothing and leaves the machine as found; and the evidence behind the original ruling says the case is rare — 1,700 sampled offers on one provider were Ubuntu and nothing else. **Due:** the Creator, if a real user ever meets the refusal on a machine that would plainly have worked. Admitting them widens what the user is promised, so it is a Specs change and theirs.

- **Six Design documents file an answer with no question above it.** *(Left by the 2026-08-25 sweep that renamed the *Left for later* heading in all nine.)*

  The heading is fixed everywhere and now says that nothing under it is open. **What was fixed in only three of the nine is the bullets themselves**: in `Servers`, `Server Setup` and `Settings` each bullet now names the question it settles, which none of them did once the questions above them were deleted. `Dependency Upload & Remap`, `Keys`, `Render Settings` and `Transfers` still carry bullets beginning *Settled at Design (2026-08-23).* with nothing saying what was settled.

  **Only three were done because only three were asked for**, and inferring the other documents' questions is work for a reader of those documents rather than for a sweep. It reads as an answer to a question the reader has to reconstruct — the same fault, in a milder form, since the heading no longer claims the thing is open.

  **Due:** at each document's own audit round, or whenever anyone is next in that section.


- **Reporting a problem has entered the Specs and Architecture has answered nothing of it.** *(Entered Specs 2026-08-25. Its Design half is under **Due now** above; this is the far half.)*

  `Preparation/Specs/Reporting a problem.md` promises four things this stage has never been asked about: **a rolling record of Smoozy's own acts** on the user's computer, capped at 20 MB with the oldest lines dropped; **a mark written at the moment the user asks to quit**, whose absence at the next launch is how a crash is recognised; **a slice of that record cut for a report**, dropping from the middle and saying how much it dropped; and **a file written and the folder holding it opened**, which is the first file Smoozy authors and writes.

  **Three things are already known to bite.** The mark is a **named exemption to `State, identity and recovery` D6**, whose third sentence forbids a launch-time path that differs — it needs a decisions-record line in that document saying so, per Rule 7. The **computer identity is not covered** for an exported record: `Security and licence` §13 rule 4 binds *any log* and nothing wider, so either a bundle rule says it or rule 4 widens. And **no adapter service that opens a local folder is named** in `Desktop app and delivery` §21's list, while A1 makes a module that opens one for itself a broken boundary — a gap `Download` H7 already had and nobody had noticed.

  **Two things are marked unresolved in the spec rather than promised**, and Architecture inherits both: what an operating system permits a dying process to write, and whether Blender's own crash file carries values out of the machine's environment, which `Security and licence` §13 rule 2 forbids capturing.

  **Done, 2026-08-25.** All four promises are answered, in a document that did not exist when this item was written: `Preparation/Architecture/Reporting a problem.md`, Rows 451 to 469 with 475 and 477 to 479. **The three things this item said would bite all bit exactly as written, and all three are closed.** D6 now carries the named exemption at one field and one reader; `Security and licence` §13 rule 4 is widened past *any log* to name the diary, a message and any exported record; and `Desktop app and delivery` §21 gained **two** new services — a diary service and a folder-reveal service — the second of which also closes the gap `Download` H7 had carried since it was written. **Of the two marked unresolved, one is answered and one is not.** What a dying process may write is **Fact 129**, answered on all three systems on 2026-08-25 — and on the Mac a force-quit turns out to be catchable, so Smoozy gets to write its last line. Blender's crash file is **Fact 131**, still open, and **Row 463 closes unresolved with the promise standing**: the spec's own instruction not to take the crash file until it is answered was followed rather than argued with. It rides free on the machine batch already owed.


- **Two small things from the twelfth and last mockup seam, 2026-08-24, neither a fault.**

  **Nothing draws what happens when releasing a computer cannot reach the internet.** `Preparation/Specs/Access.md` says releasing needs internet access and that the user is told so when there is none. It cannot appear on the offer screen, which is only reached by a check that succeeded, so the other home is Settings — whose seam closed as round 11. **Worth one look rather than a round.**


  Superseded by the Creator’s decision of 2026-10-04; recorded 2026-10-26. Paid-layer work is withdrawn. Earlier rulings and closures remain as history.

  **The queue banner borrows Access's `ax-why` class** for a disabled control's reason. It renders correctly, and it is only a tidiness point — but `Preparation/Design/Mockup/About this mockup.md` says twice that a screen's classes take a prefix of their own, and that reusing a name has already gone wrong twice in ways that do not look broken. The shape now serves at least two modules and could take a neutral name.

  **Due:** at the Design close, or whenever anyone is next in either place.


- **Four things from the `Keys` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, none of them that round's to fix.**

  **The document names the wrong one of the three underway shapes.** `Preparation/Design/Keys.md` calls the checking row's mark *"the whole-app underway on the control shape, at its smallest"*, and it is not on a control — no control was pressed, the screen simply opened. `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s addition of 2026-08-24 covers this case exactly: where the control that started the work is gone, the reading carries the mark. **That round left the sentence more visibly wrong than it found it**, having moved the mark, which is the reason to park it rather than forget it. **Due:** at `Keys.md`'s own audit round, which has never run.

  **The Servers first-run guidance links into Keys in its picking shape.** A user following the empty-list steps lands on a Keys screen that is choosing a key for a server, when no server is being chosen for. **The link belongs to the Servers screen**, closed as round 4, though the wrong shape shows up on Keys. The unnamed shape that round drew gives it a cleaner landing than it had. **Due:** at the Design close, or whenever the Servers screen is next opened.

  **The `KEYS — not designed` banner is still in the drawing**, above a screen that has been designed for weeks. The round's boundary names it as the mockup's own machinery and ruled it out as a finding, correctly. It is a leftover. **Due:** whenever anyone is next in that part of the file.

  **A want: moving the file-naming settlement into *Making a key*.** It sits in the section holding what this document left open, which was renamed on 2026-08-25 and now says plainly that nothing under it is still open — so the worst of this is gone. What remains is that a settled decision is filed away from the material it belongs with. **Due:** at `Keys.md`'s own audit round.


- **Two sentences about `--ink3` cannot both stand.** *(Noted by an auditor of the `Dependency Upload & Remap` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, and kept out of that round by its boundary.)*

  `Preparation/Design/Dependency Upload & Remap.md`'s state table glosses `--ink3` as *a value Smoozy cannot confirm*. `Preparation/Design/Design values.md` says the opposite twice: `--ink3` is ground and ink and carries no meaning, and *"A value Smoozy cannot confirm is shown by the italic face and the dash, not by a colour of its own. Nothing is reserved for it."*

  **The drawing was repaired and the gloss was not.** That round repainted the rows to `--ink3` as the document has it, which is the seam fix; whether the sentence beside it is right belongs to `Design values`, whose round closed as round 2.

  **Due:** at the Design close, or at either document's own audit round. Nothing draws wrongly.


- **`Whole app.md` uses a dependency search as its example of the underway shape that covers a pane, and that panel does not cover its pane.** *(Same round, deliberately not filed as a finding.)*

  `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` gives *over the pane* as the shape for work that stops everything on the pane, and names a dependency search running as an example. **The document and the drawing for that panel agree it stays fully usable while the search runs** — which is the right answer under the whole-app rule that the narrowest true shape wins.

  **It is a document against a document rather than against a drawing**, which is why the seam round was the wrong place to close it.

  **Due:** at `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s own audit round, or at the Design close.


- **Three faults inside `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md`, for that document's own round.** *(Found by an auditor of its mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, and kept out of that round by its boundary — a seam round does not judge a document on its own terms.)*

  **Its *Removing a Blender* section is two sentences against the richest dialog on the screen.** The drawing has had to invent that box's title, its count line and its layout with nothing to check them against. The seam round added a third sentence and did not close this.

- **The running setup card has no way back to the panel that started it.** *(Left open by the `Server Setup` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24. Neither auditor raised it; the Verdict noticed it while writing two other fixes and did not invent an answer.)*

  That round took the step list off the running card and made the card's own action unavailable while the setup runs. So a user who closed the setup panel has nothing on the card that re-opens it. **No document settles what should happen**, which is why nothing was drawn.

  **Due:** at `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md`'s own audit round, or at the Design close. It blocks nothing — the setup runs to its end either way, and the panel reports when it does.


- **A want, weighed rather than built: which build a live job is using, shown in the version table.** *(Dropped from the `Server Setup` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, because nobody had decided it.)*

  The drawing carried a line under a version saying a render was using it. It came out because no document settles that the table says anything about live work, and Design does not invent product. **It may be worth deciding** — a user about to remove a Blender has an obvious reason to want it.

  **Due:** the Creator, whenever they want to weigh it. Under Rule 7 it enters at Design if it only changes what the screen shows, and at Specs if it changes what the user is promised.


- **The connect control has four readings and the Servers screen needs a fifth.** *(From the `Servers` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24. The finding stands; the fix does not belong to that round.)*

  `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` gives the control four readings: filled green connected, hollow not connected, amber while Smoozy repairs a dropped connection, and red and unavailable where a new key is needed. **A server Smoozy has just restarted is none of the four**, and the drawing reads *Restarting* on an amber control.

  **The drawing was left as it is, deliberately.** The two honest endings were a hollow *Connect* on a restarting row, or a fifth reading in the whole-app list. The whole-app list belongs to a round that closed the day before, and the nearest question the plan has answered points at leaving it: connect is **withheld** where it is certain to fail, which is exactly a machine Smoozy has just taken down. Amber is also inside what `Preparation/Design/Design values.md` gives that colour.

  **What is owed is one clause** in the whole-app list, naming the fifth reading.

  **Due:** at `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s own audit round, or at the Design close. It blocks nothing and nothing is drawn wrongly.

  In `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html`: `.a.inl` sits inside the Servers block with a comment explaining what it is for and no element on the screen using it, and `.svhint` painted the footer hint that round took out. Neither was on the must-do list, so the implementer left both rather than widening its own brief — which is the right instinct, and it is why they are written here instead.

  **Due:** whenever anyone next edits that block, or at the Design close. Nothing draws wrongly either way.


- **Four faults inside `Preparation/Design/Servers.md`, for that document's own round.** *(Found by the auditors of its mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, and kept out of that round by its boundary — a seam round does not judge a document on its own terms.)*

  **The dropped-setup paragraph contradicts itself.** It quotes the act as *Connect without setting it up, and forget the setup* and then says *"The menu item is for dropping it **without** connecting."* The seam round drew the wording the document quotes and left the muddle alone.

  **When the blank-field line appears is answered twice**, in different words — one sentence sets a floor, the other names a moment. They do not actually contradict, which is why nothing was changed.

  **Due:** at that document's own audit round, or at the Design close.


- **The too-small message fires at exactly the width it calls enough.** *(Noticed by an auditor of the `Whole app` mockup seam, 2026-08-24, filed in its general thoughts rather than as a finding, and left standing by the runner at the round close.)*

  `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html` shows the message at 680 pixels and under, and the message itself reads *Drag it out to at least 680 pixels across*. So at exactly 680 Smoozy is saying the window is too small while naming that width as enough. `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` says the message appears **below** the floor.

  **One of the two comparisons is off by a pixel and it does not matter which.** Either the message fires under 680 rather than at it, or it names a width one greater than the floor. It is a pixel at one width and nobody would meet it by accident.

  **Due:** whenever anyone next edits the narrowing steps, or at the Design close.


- **A spinner is declared under a comment about something else.** *(Noticed by an auditor of the `Whole app` mockup seam, 2026-08-24, and left standing by the runner at the round close.)*

  In `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html`, the comment about clearing a failure is followed immediately by `.ckdot`, which is the spinner About's *Checking…* line uses. What the comment describes is `.fx`, three rules further down. Nothing is broken and nothing draws wrongly; the rule is filed under the wrong heading, which is the kind of thing that makes the next reader believe a decision they were not told.

  **Due:** whenever anyone next edits that part of the stylesheet, or at the Design close.


- **One radius literal was left behind, and it now sits one line under the token that replaced its twin.** *(Disclosed by the implementer of the `Design values` mockup seam, 2026-08-24, and left standing by the runner at the round close.)*

  That round's fix listed eight hand-written radii to replace with their token. `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html`'s `.agroup .mini:last-child { border-radius: 0 6px 6px 0; }` is a ninth, and the rule directly above it — `.agroup .mini:first-child` — was in the eight and now reads `var(--r-md) 0 0 var(--r-md)`. So two adjacent rules describing one control's two ends disagree about how a radius is written.

  **It was left on purpose.** A close check confirms the must-do list was carried out and nothing beyond it was added, and adding a ninth would have been the implementer widening its own brief.

  **Due:** whenever anyone next edits that file's values, or at the Design close. It blocks nothing and nothing looks wrong on screen — both ends still draw 6px.


- **One queue entry shows six statements about what a job writes, and two of them cannot both be true.** *(Noticed by the implementer of the Queue & History mockup seam, 2026-08-24, disclosed rather than fixed, and left standing by the runner at the round close.)*

  `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html` draws *Writing into `beauty/`, `mist/`, `previews/` and the job folder — 720 files* directly above *This job writes no files at all*. They contradict each other outright.

  **It is drawn that way on purpose and the drawing already does it elsewhere.** The state is the showcase entry — the one reached by *A job with marks on it* — and it exists to show every statement at once so they can be looked at. The *About this job* block above it shows all six marks the same way, and the same entry reads *Nothing has rendered yet* beside 62% progress. So the entry is a catalogue rather than a claim about one job.

  **What is owed is not a redrawing.** It is that nothing on the entry, and nothing in `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md`, says it is a catalogue — so the next person to read that screen meets a flat contradiction with no sign it is deliberate. Either the drawing says so where it can be seen, or the state is split, or the document records the convention. The cheapest of the three is a sentence in the document.

  **Due:** at that document's own audit round, or at the Design close. It blocks nothing, and the entry is not a screen any user reaches — every real entry carries only the statements true of it.


- **The whole-app design hides half its own failure convention, and looks to have lost some headings.** *(Filed by the auditors of the Design seam round on what a failure looks like, 2026-08-24, as belonging to that document's own round rather than to a seam.)* Two halves, both in `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`. **The convention is split across two headings and the second is *What this document does not settle*** — *Where a failure appears* holds the rules about where and when, while the shape itself, the panel and its readings sit some seventy lines further down under a heading telling a reader the material has moved elsewhere. Every screen document points at that file for its conventions, and two auditors think that is why five screens never picked the shape up. **And several headings appear to have been lost**: reading down, at least three sections run on under the wrong one — the failure shape and its panel, the update check in About, and the notifications list, which begins mid-thought. Whatever restructuring happened on 2026-08-23 seems to have dropped headings rather than text.

  **Due:** at that document's own audit round, or sooner if anyone is editing its structure anyway. It blocks nothing, but it is the likeliest cause of the next screen missing a convention.

- **Three documents still say no screen has settled its keyboard shortcuts, and all nine have.** *(Filed by an auditor of the Design seam round on what a failure looks like, 2026-08-24, as a seam but not that one.)* `Preparation/Design/Method.md`, `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md` and `Preparation/Design/Render Settings.md` each describe as open something `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s *Each screen's keys* section and every screen's own *Keyboard shortcuts* section settled on 2026-08-23. It is three sentences, and it will make the board's count wrong for anyone who trusts them.

  **Due: done, 2026-08-24.** All three sentences now say the shortcuts are settled and point at where.

- **`--bad` has no fill token, and the values document's own rule says it should.** *(Filed by an auditor of the Design seam round on what a failure looks like, 2026-08-24.)* `Preparation/Design/Design values.md` makes it a rule that a colour used both for written text and for filled shapes carries two tokens — `--busy` / `--busy-fill`, `--accent` / `--accent-fill`. `--bad` paints the failure dot, which is a filled shape, and has one token. Either the pair is completed or the rule says why this one is exempt.

  **Due:** at that document's own audit round. It blocks nothing.

  **The `Design values` mockup seam met it and left it parked, 2026-08-24.** Neither auditor reached it on that round's evidence, so it was not that round's work. Two things the round changed about it, both worth knowing when it is answered: `--bad` no longer paints a transfer tag, which was one instance of the question, and it still paints a failed job's status dot, which is another — and the same round settled that the three other endings' dots take the fill where the colour has one, which leaves the failed dot the odd one out.

- **Two failures were given the whole-app shape without settling whether they carry a cross.** *(Left open by the implementer of the Design seam round on what a failure looks like, 2026-08-24, because the Verdict did not ask and settling it would have gone beyond the must-do.)* The shape puts a cross only on a failure describing something still true. `Preparation/Design/Keys.md`'s four key failures and `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md`'s failed step both now take the shape, and neither document says which side of that line they fall on. An unusable key is arguably still true until the key is mended; a step that stopped is arguably a recorded ending.

  **Due: done, 2026-08-24. Neither carries a cross, and the drawing did force it.** An unusable key cleared would look like a key that was never there, which is the confusion the spec requires the row to prevent — so the two ways out stand where a cross would, and the message ends when the key is usable again. A step that stopped is a recorded ending of a finished run rather than a condition still going on, so there is nothing standing to clear and the panel's own two controls end it. **Re-ruled 2026-08-29, and the outcome did not move.** The Design sitting that worked through Architecture's send-backs found this entry's reasoning built on a premise Architecture had since closed — `Server Setup` S6's clearing set — and reversed the premise while keeping the conclusion: **still no cross, for the corrected reason.** *Ignore* was renamed **Close** in both part-way dialogs, so this entry's own wording named a control that no longer exists. **Recorded rather than smoothed:** a ruling that survives its own reasoning being replaced is worth one line saying so, because the next reader would otherwise take the old premise as still standing. Written into `Preparation/Design/Keys.md` and `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md`.

- **The Whole app spec says *the machine* where every screen says *server*.** *(Rejected as a finding in the Design seam round on what a failure looks like, 2026-08-24, because the fix edits a spec and Rule 10 closed the specs to Design. Parked because the drift is real.)* `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` says the raw output is *"Blender's, or whatever the machine said"*, and `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`'s words rule is firm that *"The rented computer is a server… Never machine."* The screen label already obeys the rule — *What the server said* — so nothing is built wrong; it is one word of a spec's reasoning, whenever the two are wanted to read alike.

  **Due:** the Creator, whenever they want it tidy. It blocks nothing, and it joins the two other one-sentence spec tidyings already parked here.

- **The project's record of three sites refusing automated retrieval is stale for at least one of them.** *(Raised by the architecture runner, 2026-08-23, out of the 6.8 re-run repair.)* `packages.debian.org` was read normally by **two separate implementers on the same day**, one of them supplying the file listing behind Fact 39's Linux correction and the other the Essential tags behind `Server Setup` R1. The project's note that it refuses is what caused both claims to be left unsourced in the first place, and one of them had been carried as *stated as read with no record and not even a date*. **What this costs is not the two claims, which are now sourced** — it is that a standing note saying a source cannot be reached will park the next question too. **Due: at the stage close**, when the note's other two sites are checked the same way and the note is corrected or removed.

- ~~**A real long-transfer endurance run is still owed; the existing evidence does not cover it.**~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-22 — the run was taken, in full, and it is written up and recorded; marked here 2026-09-02, at the Architecture stage close.** It ran on the rented machine A of the multi-provider batch, from 11:16 UTC to the download's publication at 15:12:11 UTC — about **three hours fifty-six minutes**. The fixture was an **allocated deterministic 4 294 967 296-byte** file, built by the same algorithm at both ends, byte-identical, and `st_blocks x 512 == st_size` at both ends, so not sparse. It was moved **in both directions by Smoozy's own byte loop**, held deliberately at **384.0 KB/s** on the upload. **Four independent digests agreed** — source at rest, pass-through, staged-at-rest read back over the subsystem, and the machine's own `sha256sum`, a program this project did not write. **Two forced process kills and a killed serving connection were exercised**, and in all three endings the final name was absent, checked from the machine; the record was found **behind** the disk after a kill and **ahead** of it after a drop, which is why the resume takes the smaller of the two. **A mid-flight cap change was run four times**, across two rate governors. Memory held flat at **64 416 KB**, open file descriptors at **10**, and publication took **0.139 s** by `posix-rename`, the rename being the cleanup. The write-up is [The multi-provider batch](../Architecture/The%20multi-provider%20batch%20%E2%80%94%20the%20surface%20that%20did%20not%20vary,%20the%20Debian%20route%20that%20exists,%20and%20the%20cards%20a%20slice%20cannot%20see.md), *The endurance run — the Creator's own*, and it is recorded **CLOSED** at [Server protocol and transfers](../Architecture/Server%20protocol%20and%20transfers.md#12-unresolved) §12 item 4 — which also records that **S15, S16, S20, S22 and S26's Published row are observed rather than reasoned**, and which carries the two corrections the run earned: the write path must be pipelined (**R6a**) and the rate's averaging window must be bounded (**R6b**). **What the write-up does not record**, so nobody reads more into this than was done: CPU and disk were not observed, and neither a long network loss nor an application exit and fresh run was posed — the two process kills and the killed connection are what the ending classes rest on. ~~The original entry follows.~~ *(Recorded at the Creator's request, 2026-08-21.)* The project has moved and digest-checked a real **669,966,622-byte** Blender file, proved exact resume in both directions, and exercised its own **400 MiB** transfer loop over a real link. It has also measured rate caps, drops, corruption handling and several providers. **It has not transferred a dense 4 GiB file and has not held one planned transfer open for roughly three hours.** The 500 GB sparse fixture used to test listing behaviour is not transfer evidence. **The later run must use an allocated deterministic 4 GiB file and the planned Smoozy mechanism in both directions; deliberately hold at least one direction near 350–400 KB/s; digest the source, receiving file at rest, and final file; exercise clean resume, process death, a long network loss and connection replacement, application exit/new run, a mid-flight cap change, publication and cleanup; observe memory, CPU, file descriptors and disk; and prove that no partial ever appears under the final name.** ~~**Due:** when a suitable rental batch is proposed, preferably combined with the outstanding Blender facts and targeted server coverage. **No rental or spend is authorised by this entry.**~~ **Discharged 2026-08-22 by the batch above.**

- **A targeted multi-provider compatibility batch needs its matrix and spending limit proposed and approved.** *(Recorded at the Creator's request, 2026-08-21.)* This is not a request to repeat the broad work already done: the project has exercised multiple rentals and provider labels, Ubuntu 22.04 and 24.04, VM and constrained-container shapes, root and non-root logins, direct and proxied SSH, several NVIDIA GPUs and driver conditions, attached volumes, full disks, reboot/recycle behaviour and real transfers. **First settle the product's supported Linux distribution and CPU-architecture boundary.** Then select only missing high-value axes: genuinely independent providers; managed direct SSH versus marketplace/proxy; VM versus container and, only if promised, bare metal; a current GPU/driver family; and any supported non-x86 or non-NVIDIA case. Run one unchanged probe on every box for login/elevation, boot return, init system, filesystem and ownership, SSH/SFTP/session behaviour, GPU/driver state, package tools, resource limits, persistence, worker stop and transfer behaviour. **Before renting, an agent must show the exact machines, questions and expected total; the approved spending limit must not be exceeded without a new ruling. Destroy every rental and detachable volume afterward and verify the accounts are empty.** **Due:** after the second sanity-sweep verification identifies the real support boundary and machine-dependent claims. **No rental or spend is authorised now.**

- **Three more drafted Design sentences the architecture bounds rather than supplies, all from `Preparation/Design/File Browser.md`.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Rows 342 and 344, 2026-08-21.)* **An unreadable folder *"greyed, saying why"* before it is opened** — `Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md` P2a keeps the sentence's own stated purpose whole (the folder always stays in the list, because seeing a name in the parent is a different permission from reading into it) and establishes that the marking cannot be done: L1 and X1 both forbid the per-entry probe, **and a free probe would not answer the question anyway**, since *can this login read into this folder* is not answered by what the machine says about the entry. **And *"with the offer to try again with admin access beside it"*** — P5 makes the offer conditional, and **on a container under a non-root login there is no offer at all**, so a refusal shown with nothing beside it is a real state the design has no drawing for. **And the live-job warning's *"may finish anyway with the wrong picture in it"*** — W5a bounds it: uniform across files (`Dependency Upload & Remap` V8), differentiated across jobs (W5), so the clause survives as a uniform *may* and **naming the picture case as the consequence is a per-kind claim V8 forbids**. **None is a Specs question and none went to the Creator.** ~~**Due:** when Design resumes, or at the Design close.~~ **Due: done, 2026-08-24.** The third was corrected on 2026-08-23 — the live-job warning says *with something wrong in it*. The other two closed at the `File Browser` mockup seam, round 1, which is where Design resumed on that document. **The unreadable folder is no longer marked before it is opened**: `Preparation/Design/File Browser.md` now says Smoozy cannot tell in advance and that opening the folder is what produces the answer, with the greying arriving alongside the refusal rather than standing ahead of it. **And the offer is conditional on both sides** — the document says it sits beside the refusal *where the machine allows one*, and the drawing gained the twin state where a refusal shows with nothing beside it, which is the container-under-a-non-root-login case the architecture names and nothing had ever drawn. All three rows are answered either way.

The restart sentence stands as drawn. Both rows are answered either way.

- **A drafted Design sentence the architecture's own evidence contradicts: *the frame in hand is lost*.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Row 353, 2026-08-21.)* `Preparation/Design/Servers.md` has *stop and clear first* naming what it costs, and one of its two clauses is *"the frame in hand is lost"*. **`Preparation/Architecture/Servers.md` W19a establishes that the outcome is one of exactly two and that nobody can know which before the act**: the termination is an interrupt, and an interrupt landing mid-render leaves no file for that frame while an interrupt landing during that frame's own write **finishes the write and leaves the frame whole** — which is the one case anybody has measured, across hundreds of runs in every version on both machines. **So the statement architecture can stand behind is *may not arrive*, not *is lost*.** The sentence is marked a **drafted design sentence** on its row, which is exactly the case that marking exists for. **Nothing about what the user gets changes** — only what a sentence claims about it — so this is not a Specs question and did not go to the Creator. ~~**Due:** when Design resumes, or at the Design close, whichever comes first.~~ **Due: done, 2026-08-24**, at the `Servers` mockup seam, round 1 — which is where Design resumed on that sentence. **It was on both sides of the seam**, the drawing saying *"The frame it is on is lost"* in the removal box and the document saying it in prose, so both were changed. **The wording is *may not be saved*, not this note's own *may not arrive*.** Rule 9 binds anything on a screen and its test is that vague is the worse failure: in Smoozy a frame also *arrives* by being downloaded, so *may not arrive* on that box could be read as being about the download rather than about whether the file exists on the server at all. Row 353 is answered either way.

- **Three more drafted Design elements the architecture bounds rather than supplies, all from the Settings cluster.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Rows 330, 332 and 334, 2026-08-21.)* **`Preparation/Design/Settings.md`'s *"A folder that cannot be read stays on the list, marked **not found**"*** — `Preparation/Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` F1a keeps both of the sentence's own purposes for nothing (the list is durable and only the user removes a row; the rest are searched anyway, which is F7 unchanged) and refuses the standing mark: F7's verdict is **one walk's and run-held**, so a row carries it only in the run a search ran in, and **a probe when the screen is opened would not answer the question either** — it covers the listed folder while F2 makes the folder its whole subtree. The verdict also **does not separate missing, moved and refused**, so a word claiming absence over-claims. **And the same document's *"The finished-job switch says it carries the frame"*** — `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` T17a establishes that the picture is **a common case rather than a property**, absent four ways that are none of them faults (the macOS 10 MB ceiling with nothing establishing a derivative fits inside it, the Linux silent drop, the Windows contradiction, and T17's refusal to wait on a fetch), so a label names the kind and states the picture only as the spec does — *where the job has one* — or not at all. **And `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html` line 2109's licence panel, `SMZ-••••-••••-4C7A`** — the new sixth binding rule under `Preparation/Architecture/Security and licence.md` §3.0 makes a mask that preserves any part of the value the value, and **nothing anywhere supplies a display fragment of a stored secret**. The same mockup already masks the Telegram token and the server password correctly, and those stay as drawn provided the dot count is not derived from the value. **None is a Specs question and none went to the Creator** — `Access` never says the user is shown a key back, and the other two spec sentences survive whole. ~~**Due:** when Design resumes, or at the Design close.~~ **Two are done, 2026-08-24.** The licence panel is masked whole in both places, since a mask keeping four characters of the value is the value, and the search-folder row now reads *could not be read at 09:20* — the honest word, and dated to the search that met it rather than standing, because nothing probes a folder when the screen opens. ~~**The finished-job switch's label is still owed.**~~ **All three are done. The third was already done and this sentence had gone stale** — confirmed at the `Settings` mockup seam, round 1, 2026-08-24, which read both sides against each other with the auditors not told the item existed. `Preparation/Design/Settings.md` states the picture only as the spec does, *where the job has one*, with the reasoning and the Rule 10 label; the drawing reads *with the frame, where the job has one*. **Nothing was owed and nothing was changed.** All three rows are answered either way.

- **A Design document that disagrees with its own mockup, and the architecture supports the mockup.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Row 402, 2026-08-21.)* `Preparation/Design/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` says the *what the file records* column carries *"beneath it the scene using it"* — **singular** — while `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html` draws a scene line reading *Interior, Exterior*. **`Preparation/Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` G20a supports the plural and had to**: the walk carries on until every scene reachable from a reference has been reached, because **a row naming one of two scenes reads exactly like a row naming the only one**, and the spec's own use for the line is a user setting irrelevant rows aside by hand — so a truncated attribution drops an asset a scene they *are* rendering needs, at success, silently. **Nothing is unsupported here**; the two Design statements disagree with each other and reconciling the wording is Design's. **Not a Specs question and it did not go to the Creator.** **Due: done, 2026-08-24.** The document says *every scene using it* and carries the reason, which is that a row naming one of two scenes reads exactly like a row naming the only one. Row 402 is answered either way.

- **A drafted Design sentence that holds on one route into a screen and not on the other.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Row 423, 2026-08-21.)* `Preparation/Design/Keys.md`, under *Opened from a server dialog*: *"**The screen says which server it is choosing for.** A list of keys with no context is the same screen as the housekeeping one, and the user arrived at it in the middle of something."* **It holds coming from Edit Server**, where the saved row's label exists. **It cannot be kept coming from Add Server**, where the user may open Keys from a dialog whose name, username and host are all still empty and **no value naming the server exists anywhere** — the row does not exist, and a label is a field of a saved row. `Preparation/Architecture/Keys.md` U11 hands over the dialog's own fields as they stand and **nothing where there is none**; **composing a name from anything else was declined as the escalation**, since it would put on screen a name true of nothing. **Not a Specs question** — no spec promises the sentence — **and it did not go to the Creator.** Narrowing the wording is Design's. ~~**Due:** when Design resumes, or at the Design close.~~ **Due: done, 2026-08-24**, at the `Keys` mockup seam, round 1 — which is when Design resumed on that screen. **Both sides moved**: the document's sentence is narrowed to the route that has a name, and **the drawing gained the shape it had never had** — opened from Add Server, where nothing names the server, the line reads *Choosing a key* and stops. **Nothing composes a name**, which is what the architecture declined and what would have put on screen a name true of nothing. Row 423 is answered either way.

- **A drawn mockup value the architecture refuses outright: *idle in 12 min* on a queue server heading.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from Row 424, 2026-08-21.)* `Preparation/Design/Mockup/Smoozy.html` draws it on the `Vast 5060` group heading, where every other reading is a statement about **now**. It is a **forecast over a whole server's line**, and `Preparation/Architecture/Queue & History.md` **B9** had already declined the strictly smaller version — a countdown over one job — closing with *"the row where nothing is offered is the answer"*. **W1a refuses the wider figure and works the case rather than leaning on B9**: it is three terms, of which B9's is the first; **the second has no operand at all**, an unstarted job having no elapsed time, no counted frames and no estimate of Blender's, with B1's plan total needing a units-per-second rate nothing in this stage measures; **and the line is not a fixed quantity**, since held jobs wait on a person, the user may reorder at any moment, and **untracked work counts as work** — a machine rendering a declined job is busy and Smoozy holds no manifest, no plan total and no reading for it. **So any forecast is short by an amount it can neither bound nor state**, and B9's harm becomes that error summed over the line on the one heading standing for the machine. **What the heading can carry instead is what it already draws** — connected, what is rendering, what is waiting — **plus W1 to W5's *has been idle and for how long*; the mockup's own shut-state sibling, *Nothing rendering · 4 waiting*, is exactly the supported shape.** **Not a Specs question and it did not go to the Creator**, and `About this mockup.md`'s own rule is that a drawing moves when it disagrees with the documents. **Due: done, 2026-08-24.** The heading carries what it can read now — what is rendering and what is waiting — which is the shape its own shut-state sibling already drew. Row 424 is answered either way.

- **The user-facing fallback re-run could read the free space before it issues the plain start.** *(Parked by the architecture runner from `Server Setup`'s Round 1, 2026-08-19, where an auditor raised it and typed it a want himself.)* `Server Setup` §9b step 3 issues one plain start on one condition by one route, deliberately, and the same auditor's leave-alone list defends that boundedness in the same audit. The idea is that a machine whose disk is full will fail the start for a reason the re-run could have named first, so the user is told *it did not start* where they could have been told *there is no room*. **Against it:** the disk-full negative is inherited and already ruled, the step would grow a second variant and a second failure mode of its own, and nothing establishes that the reading is cheap or reliable on a machine already in that state. **Due:** whenever the fallback re-run is next opened — a seam round, the Build plan for that module, or a Creator ruling on what the re-run says. It blocks nothing. **FIRED 2026-09-04, at Build's step 6 close, which is when that module was built — one of the three moments this Due names. Marked so the trigger is not left reading as future, and confirmed by opening `App/Server Setup/src/fallback.rs`**, where step 3 still issues the plain start on one condition by one route and reads no free space before it. **Nothing in step 6 or its audit round touched it, and it still blocks nothing.** **Due, re-pointed:** the Creator, or whenever `Server Setup` §9b is next opened.

- **The local record has a record kind nobody's inventory names.** `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` puts a durable per-transfer progress record in the local record — the transfer identity, direction, server, installation, final and staging paths, expected size, checkpointed offset with its digest, the run that last wrote it, and the ending — while `Preparation/Architecture/State, identity and recovery.md` §1.1's own inventory of that record names only *transfer arrangements*. Round 1 ruled it a defect on one narrow point only, and that half is repaired: the Subject now says outright that it is adding a kind the inventory does not name, and files the seam as its own §12 item 13. **The inventory half was deliberately not repaired**, because writing it from the other document would give one fact two homes. *(From the Round 1 close on Server protocol and transfers, 2026-08-17.)*

  **This entry is the one place the tally is kept, and it is now seven documents.** It was counted three different ways in three files, which is its own small evidence of the problem; from 2026-08-19 the number is here and nowhere else. **The seven are the documents that record the seam in an unresolved list of their own**, in order of finding: `Server protocol and transfers` §12 item 13 (2026-08-17); `Job files and outputs` §14 item 3 and `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 5, which are the machine-store inventory of §1.2 rather than §1.1's and are the same shape; `Desktop app and delivery` §22 item 14; `Dependency Upload & Remap` §12 item 15 (2026-08-18); **`Queue & History` §18 item 13 (2026-08-19)** — its catch-up notice record and its idle-spoken mark are named by no inventory either; and **`Telegram` §21 item 14 (2026-08-19), which makes seven** — the desktop's told-record of what it last told each machine, which §1.1 names nowhere though §3 D1's own table already requires it for the lock policy, and the Telegram worker's outstanding-instruction records, which §1.2's contents line does not carry. *(The earlier tally also counted `The shape` and `Security and licence` §9, and this one does not. Neither records a seam of its own anywhere, and §1.1's inventory as it now stands names server rows, key rows and remembered machine-derived values, which is where §9's two sensitive-but-not-secret things land. Dropped 2026-08-19 by the corrections re-check, which is also what made the list and the number agree.)* **A pattern this deep is no longer a document's oversight**: whoever settles this should ask whether §1.1's inventory is meant to be a closed list at all, rather than adding two more kinds to it and waiting for the eighth.

  ~~**Due:** at the seam check between those documents, or sooner if `State, identity and recovery` is opened for any other reason.~~ **CLOSED 2026-08-22 at the Architecture seam close, and finished off twice since; marked 2026-09-02.** **The inventory was widened by the document that owns it, which is what this entry always said had to happen.** `State, identity and recovery` **J3** — added 2026-08-22, routed by the runner from `Dependency Upload & Remap` §12 item 15 and `Queue & History` §18 item 13 — names the missing kinds in §1.1's own row and states outright that **nothing about their shape or contents is decided there**, each staying settled in the document that owns it. **J12**, added the same day, does the same for §1.2 from `Job files and outputs` §14 item 3 and `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 5. **The per-transfer progress record is in the row too**, as *per-transfer records nested under their arrangement or multi-part action outcome, including cleanup debts*. **Two later sittings finished it:** **J4** on 2026-08-25 added four more durable things, and **I5** on 2026-08-29 added Smoozy's own temporary area. **And the deeper question this entry asked was answered rather than dodged:** §1.1's inventory is written as **the complete list of what this side holds**, naming each kind and deferring its shape to the owning document — so it is a list that grows by routing rather than a closed enumeration waiting for an eighth exception. **The tally stops here at seven.**

- **Architecture's Windows gap now has a named list, and only a Windows computer closes any of it.** The Creator's own computer is a Mac, and this stage has never had a **desktop** Windows computer or a Linux desktop computer. **It has had a Windows computer since 2026-08-26** — a Kamatera rental, used four times and worked on three — but **every one of them booted a Server edition**, whatever its image was labelled, and both of that account's *desktop*-labelled images do. Until 2026-08-18 this was a general caution; the reading batch of that day turned it into a specific list, and it is written here rather than left inside one input document so that a machine arriving is known to close real work rather than merely being useful. *(Corrected 2026-08-30. **Read each item below against that split**: what a Server edition can answer is now runnable and only wants a batch, while anything that turns on a desktop edition is still unreachable. The two are not the same gap and this entry treated them as one.)* **What is owed, all from `Preparation/Architecture/The user's own computer — where a download lands, what it will take, what it will show, and how the app replaces itself.md`:** whether any documented Windows call returns a file's on-disk capitalisation (a negative over thirteen Microsoft pages, and the largest hole in Fact 16); NTFS's case-folding rule, undocumented on every page read; whether an exact-comparison lookup can be had on a folding Windows volume; what Windows returns for a forbidden character, for a reserved stem, for an unpaired surrogate, and for an over-long component; whether a name that survives the long-path prefix stays reachable by ordinary Windows software; whether the downloads folder's on-disk name is localised; and what a detached volume reports on a write, where six candidate codes exist and Microsoft's own page names none. **Two more want a Linux desktop rather than a rented server:** which filesystem a distribution presents by default, and whether the kernel's case-folding option behaves as its documentation says.

  **Four more were added on 2026-08-18 by `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md`, and one of them is a numbered fact of its own.** **Fact 97** — whether Windows' reputation warning fires on a build the application downloads and launches itself, which matters there and nowhere else because the Windows update shape is side-by-side by construction and cannot take the precaution the macOS answer takes. **Whether an application may create its own Start-menu shortcut** or an installer is required for it: the shortcut is Windows' precondition for posting a notification at all, and **if an installer is required that is a change to what the user gets and a Specs question**, not a detail. **Whether the Windows publication rename behaves as the operation Fact 79 now names** — unread on both routes, and deliberately not read across from the update answer, which is about a running executable rather than a data file. And **which of two contradictory Microsoft pages governs a notification's picture**, which no reading can settle because both were verified verbatim and neither acknowledges the other. **Fact 79's Linux throwaway wants a Linux desktop** for the same reason its own entry gives: a rented Linux server does not answer for the user's own computer.

  **One more was added on 2026-08-20 by `Preparation/Architecture/Keys.md` Section 13 item 8, and it rides in this same batch.** **Whether the Windows SSH client refuses a private key whose access control is loose** — the requirement is stated, by the Windows port's own project wiki, that a private key must be owned by the user with no other user able to reach it; **the refusal is stated nowhere**, and Microsoft's own key-management page says nothing about the client refusing a private key at all, its only access-control statements being about the server. **The test is one key, one second local account granted access, one connection attempt**: does the client refuse before the passphrase question, or connect? A negative result is a connection that proceeds. **It needs a real Windows installation and a virtual machine on the Mac would do**; no rental is involved.

  **Two more were added on 2026-08-20 from the verification round, and both are ten-minute throwaways on the same machine.** **The stored capitalisation, read rather than argued** — create a file named in mixed case, enumerate its parent, print the name that comes back; a negative is a name returned folded. The documents keep saying no page documents the call, which is true, but Microsoft does state that *"the original long file name is always preserved"* on disk, so the gap is one missing word rather than a missing capability and one throwaway closes it. **And whether `Advapi32.dll` is present on a stock installation** — one line, or better one credential-writing call, which settles presence and reachability together. The challenge that Microsoft publishes a reference page for that library was checked and is false; there is no such page, only Requirements rows that state a minimum version rather than presence.

  **One more was added on 2026-08-21, and it is the smallest item in the batch.** **What the in-box Windows SSH server announces on a first connection** — one TCP connection, read the first line, no login and no change to the machine. Fact 59's Windows half is otherwise answered by reading Microsoft's own published source, which puts `OpenSSH_for_Windows_` in the identification string before authentication; what was read is the branch behind the **GitHub** build, and Microsoft distinguishes that from the **in-box** one. The marker sits in the Windows version macro rather than in the GitHub-specific suffix, so it is very likely in both, and *very likely* is not this project's bar. **This item also corrects a claim made in two documents until 2026-08-21 — that the Windows half was not posable at all and that this file recorded the gap as structural.** It was posable by reading, and this file records the Windows gap as a list of things wanting a machine rather than as anything structural.

  **What holds without any of it.** Every Windows and Linux claim in that document is documentation alone and labelled so, and Row 282 already requires every answer touching the user's own computer to be written three times. **Nothing is blocked** — an answer closes as unresolved for Windows with what breaks named, which is what `Method` provides for. What is not acceptable is an answer written once against macOS and assumed to hold.

  **A route to a Windows computer was found on 2026-08-25, and it changes this item from unschedulable to costed.** The Creator's rental account is Vast.ai, and **Vast rents no Windows at all** — its renter-facing documentation offers two Ubuntu templates and nothing else, and Windows appears only in its host-facing pages as an example of what the technology could support. Label: **documented** (`https://docs.vast.ai/instances/virtual-machines`, read 2026-08-25). **The finding that shapes everything else:** almost nobody rents *desktop* Windows by the hour, because of how Microsoft licenses it — the cheap clouds sell Windows Server with the licence included, and Windows 10 or 11 is either absent or wants a licence the renter already holds.

  **That split decides which machine answers which question, and getting it wrong would produce confident wrong answers.** Microsoft's own page lists the reputation warning as a feature of Windows Pro, Enterprise and Education and **does not list Windows Server** (`https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/operating-system-security/virus-and-threat-protection/microsoft-defender-smartscreen/`, read 2026-08-25), so **Fact 97 cannot be answered on a Server machine**. And the SSH server ships **installed but not enabled on Windows Server 2025** and **not installed at all on Windows 10 and 11** (`https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/openssh/openssh-overview`, read 2026-08-25), so the in-box-server item and the client-key-refusal item both want desktop Windows. Label: **documented** for both. Everything else this batch owes — the capitalisation reading, the forbidden character, the reserved stem, the unpaired surrogate, the over-long component, the long-path prefix, the localised downloads folder, the detached volume, and the publication rename — is the same filesystem behaviour on either, so the cheap Server machine answers all of it.

  **Two providers, and the reason there are two is the split above.** **Kamatera** is the only cheap hourly desktop Windows 10 or 11 found with a plain create-and-destroy interface: its own calculator lists both alongside the Server editions, at a licence of $10 a month against Server's $19, billed by the minute, which works out at roughly $0.030 to $0.071 an hour depending on the machine (`https://www.kamatera.com/pricing/` and `https://www.kamatera.com/knowledgebase/api-documentation/`, read 2026-08-25). **Its cost is a one-off identity check** — a short video of the Creator holding their ID — which is a human step before anything can be automated (`https://www.kamatera.com/faq/answer/what-is-the-manual-verification-process/`, read 2026-08-25). **Amazon EC2** is the cheapest dependable hourly Windows for everything else, Server only, at $0.0392 an hour for a small machine, billed by the second after a one-minute minimum (`https://aws.amazon.com/ec2/pricing/on-demand/` and the pricing feed that page loads, read 2026-08-25). **Azure has desktop Windows but gates it behind a Visual Studio subscription** (`https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/virtual-machines/windows/client-images`, read 2026-08-25). Every option found is x86-64.

  **Two things this does not settle, stated so nobody reads more into it.** Kamatera's own Windows page says a Windows machine costs the same as any other and carries no premium, which its own calculator contradicts — the calculator was taken as right and the first invoice is where that gets checked. And a dozen figures the researcher could not confirm on a provider's own page are marked as unconfirmed in its report rather than repeated here; none of them is Kamatera's or Amazon's.

  **Due:** the machine is now a purchase rather than a wait. **The Creator decides whether to buy it, and nothing here commits them.** When they do: one Kamatera desktop machine for the three items above that need desktop Windows, and one Amazon machine for the rest of the list, both destroyed afterwards the same way every rented Linux machine has been. The Linux desktop half is unchanged and still has no route.

- **A machine that permits too few sessions has no route to a second connection, and `Servers` owns it.** `Server protocol and transfers` admits every session it opens against one count with one reserved for the command channel, so a machine capped at one session has no channel left for a transfer. The route out is a second connection, and when a connection is made or replaced is `Servers`' — it is not in that document's scope as written. No auditor filed it as a defect and all three left it there. *(From the Round 1 close on Server protocol and transfers, 2026-08-17.)*

  **Due: done, 2026-08-19, by `Servers` — the trigger this item named.** `Preparation/Architecture/Servers.md` 1.4 decides it rather than deferring it: a connected period may hold **one additional connection to the same server, transfer channels only**, opened at the first refused channel admission and closed when the last transfer on it ends. It carries the same four details, the same key and the same host-trust decision, never prompts, never carries a command or a reading channel, and **the connection condition is derived from the primary connection alone**, so losing it is a transfer interruption and never a break. One is the cap — a remedy rather than a throughput dial. **What is left is one clarifying clause, or nothing:** T5's admission point is per server and the session count is per connection, so a period with two connections counts against two pools with the reserved command session on the primary alone. That reads as already permitted; `Servers` Section 16 item 10 records it as a seam either way, with its Due at a seam round or the stage close.

  **Reopened as a smaller question on 2026-08-21, and it does not undo the decision above.** The premise both documents rested on — that a second connection is *the only route* — is false, and the word is corrected in `Server protocol and transfers` T2 and in the `Servers` 1.4 sentence quoting it. One file-transfer channel can carry several files at once: the version-3 protocol draft says *"There is no limit on the number of outstanding (non-acknowledged) requests that the client may send to the server"* and identifies each request by a client-chosen id, and it was posed on the Creator's Mac on 2026-08-20 — two handles open at once, eight writes interleaved across them all sent before any reply was read, every reply status 0, each file holding only its own bytes. **So S14 is a choice with a named alternative rather than the sole possibility.** What is worth weighing is whether a machine capped that low should share a channel instead of opening a second connection, which trades T1's isolation for one fewer connection. **Nothing is blocked** — no observed machine has been met below the documented default of 10, and S14 works either way. **Due: at the architecture seam round, with the item above.**

- **`Whole app`'s general known-cause rule now has architecture written against it, and no row quotes it.** Round 1 refiled the refusal-driven disk-full message away from Row 287's carve-out and onto the spec's general rule that Smoozy says what to do where the cause is genuinely known. That rule is now load-bearing and **no row anywhere quotes it**, so the quote check that notices when a spec sentence moves cannot see it. *(Recorded by the Round 1 close gate on Server protocol and transfers, 2026-08-17.)*

  **Due:** at the stage-close re-sweep of `Whole app`, which is when a missing row is meant to be caught. It costs one row and blocks nothing.

- **Establishing that a file was whole before Smoozy ever read it.** Dropped as a **want** by Round 1 on `Server protocol and transfers`. The transfer's own digest is computed as Smoozy reads the source, so it proves a faithful copy and cannot prove the source was sound; a corrupt file copied faithfully is the user's own file rather than something that looks finished and is not. There is no known-good reference to compare a file Smoozy never wrote against, so the want has no form as well as no home. *(From the Round 1 Verdict on Server protocol and transfers, Terra's finding 1, 2026-08-17.)*

  **Due:** never, unless the Creator wants it. It is recorded so the idea is weighed here rather than reappearing as a defect in a later round.

- **The high-water-mark file has no owning document.** `Preparation/Architecture/Security and licence.md` introduces it in Section 12.3, as the local half of the defence against a wound-back clock, and it is neither the local record nor the secret store — so nothing yet says where it lives, what protects it, or what happens when it is missing. Round 1 on that document raised it and dropped it as a **want**: naming the owner is past where that Subject's job stops, and it belongs to `Access` or to `State, identity and recovery`. *(From the Round 1 close on Security and licence, 2026-08-16.)*

  **Due: done, 2026-08-20.** `Preparation/Architecture/Access.md` §3 T2 takes it, and the answer is that **there is no third local file**: the mark lives inside the licence record, in the local record, which is what keeps `State, identity and recovery` §1.1's one-state-store rule and §11.1's *"the licence record and its **clock** high-water mark *(the word `clock` was missing here until 2026-08-29; the source has carried it since the fresh reader's pass, and `Access` A1 and T2 and `Licence service` §12 carried the same trimmed string. Routed by the `Access` stage-close gate — one field had two names across four documents, and no quote check could catch it because the trimmed string still existed elsewhere.)*"* both true.
