### The Blender version list looks wrong — 5.2.2 appears twice

*Added 2026-09-27 by the build runner. Creator-stated, from a brief glance during a pass: 5.2.2 was listed twice, and the list looked "weird" beyond that.*

**What is known.** Only the duplicate was seen; the rest has not been looked at. The list is the desktop's reading of blender.org's download pages (step 8b's Blender listing fetch).

**How it is settled.** Open the version list in a throwaway copy, compare it against what blender.org publishes, and find why an entry repeats and what else is off, before changing anything.

**Added 2026-09-27, from the fix map** (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the seventh passes — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, fix-list item 9 and piece 10): **the duplicate may be the plan working as written.** The list is built from the channels blender.org publishes, `Server Setup` B6a says *"A published entry is a channel, not a build"*, and `Design/Server Setup § Installing` names each entry by its line (*Latest stable*, *Release*, *Long-term support*). **So one version published on two channels shows twice by design.** The map also found that the listing ignores the architecture each entry names. **Which of these made the Creator's duplicate is for the live reading above, and it comes before any code changes.** If it is two channels, the fix is how the two are drawn — Design's under Rule 10 — and never a filter that hides a published channel.

~~**Due:** with the next piece that touches the Set up screen or the Blender listing, and before step 10c closes.~~

**CLOSED 2026-09-27, by step 10c's fix piece 10: it was one release on two published tracks.** The live listing, read through the desktop's own code and checked against a raw fetch, publishes 5.2.2 on the `latest` track and on the `5.2lts` track, as stable and as candidate on both; every entry names the same architecture, so that was not the cause. blender.org's own download page calls the release *5.2.2 LTS*. **The fix is how it is drawn, not a filter**: one row per version in each part of the list, naming every track that publishes it, so the list reads *5.2.2 Latest stable · Long-term support* once above the rule and *5.2.2 Release candidate* once below. The Design sentence was written first, in `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md` § *Installing*. The account is `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Fix pieces 6, 9 and 10 — what landed.md`; the eighth pass checks that no row repeats.

**What else looked off was found and recorded there, not changed, because each wants a ruling rather than a builder**: *3 entries of the list could not be read* is shown under the list for three builds far below the oldest Smoozy offers; a candidate or beta row installs the finished build of that number; and *5.3.0 Unstable* cannot be fetched (`Server Setup` B5's known case).

**Those three are parked as their own item below, *Three oddities left in the Blender version list*. Added 2026-09-27 by fix piece 8's records agent.**

### Transfers lists every upload request ever kept

*Added 2026-09-27 by fix piece 8's records agent, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Fix pieces 3, 4, 5, 7 and 8 — what landed.md`. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md`'s upload requests and `Preparation/Design/Transfers.md`.*

Since fix piece 7, Transfers reads upload requests, and it shows every one the local record keeps, however old.

**What it costs.** The list grows for as long as Smoozy is used.

### The rail's dot can stay green while a job has started rendering

*Added 2026-10-07 by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's second notes, piece 13 — what landed.md`.*

The rail's dot (green connected, amber pulsing while a job renders) is no fresher than the server's queue reading, and that reading repeats only while Queue, the server list or that server's own page is on screen. On History, Transfers, Keys or Settings, the next job can start rendering while the dot stays green. The server list would say *Waiting* at that moment too, so the screens agree with each other, not with the server.

**What it costs.** A dot that lags a job's start by as long as the user stays on another screen. Fixing it means reading the queue all the time, which costs traffic on every connected server.

**Due:** if the Creator sees it on a test copy, or when the queue's reading is next opened.

### Ubuntu's own upgrades holding the package lock on a new machine

*Added 2026-09-27 by fix piece 8's records agent, from the eighth pass on Hyperstack. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server Setup.md` §13 item 7.*

Ubuntu's unattended upgrades ran for 17 minutes, starting 5 minutes after first boot, and admin steps wait up to 120 seconds for the package lock. Once fix piece 1 lands, a setup started in that stretch waits and then fails with the package system's own words.

**What it costs.** A failed first setup on a new machine, told truthfully.

**Due:** the ninth pass notes it if met. Reopen only if it is met on a real user's first setup.

### On RunPod, *Set up again* cannot bring Smoozy back after a restart — `Server Setup` F1 step 3 on a stored-field container

*Added 2026-09-27 by the records agent after the eighth-pass fixes, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the eighth pass — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, § The plan pass, the paragraph* One ruling for the runner*. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server Setup.md` F1 step 3 and `Rented-server runtime` §16 item 16.*

F1 step 3 says that on a container *"the keeper heals by itself … and no start is owed"*. On RunPod no keeper runs after a restart, because nothing ran the hook, so **the user-facing fallback the plan names cannot bring Smoozy back there**. Yet the seventh pass saw everything on RunPod's container disk survive the restart — package, pointer, Blender, the job and its frames — so a plain start of the keeper at F1 step 3's container branch would bring the queue back while the pod lives. **A hole, not a want**: the promise it serves is already written. It changes an architecture clause and it is RunPod-only. **Parked by the runner on 2026-09-27, not in that wave** (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`, the ruling after the eighth pass's fix map).

**What it costs.** On RunPod, after a restart, Smoozy's program stays down and nothing the user can press brings it back while the pod lives; since the eighth-pass fix piece 6 the screens at least say *Smoozy isn't responding.*

**Due:** at the next plan pass that opens `Server Setup` F1 or any RunPod work, where the runner rules whether it becomes a piece; and before step 10c closes.

### A newer Telegram tap refused by an older server is told as silence

*Added 2026-09-29 by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Transfer wave piece 11 — what landed.md`, *Found, and for the runner*, items 2 and 3. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` B9a, B12, R1 and R12, and for the Release Runner's step on raising the protocol version.*

`Telegram` B9a's *older version* sentence rides the acknowledgement, and B12 gives a button tap and a *how is it going* request none. So a server on an older protocol leaves such an entry alone, and the user reads the deadline's answer — *That button has expired or its server did not respond, so nothing was done.*, or the gather's *did not answer* line — which is the *went quiet* reading B9a was written to prevent. Nothing is done, so it is safe; it is not the truth B9a promises.

**What it costs, and when it bites.** Nothing today: no program on a machine reads a board yet, and every release speaks protocol version 1. **The day a release raises the protocol version, every button on every older server's messages answers that way until the server updates.** And since piece 11, raising the version is visible to older machines — every action a newer machine boards for an older one is refused — so **the Release Runner should know that raising it costs this.**

**Due:** before any release raises the protocol version, or when a machine first reads a board, whichever is first. A plan act on B9a and B12, not a builder's.

### Failure points left for later, Creator-stated

*Added 2026-09-28 by the build runner. The Creator, of the runner's list: "Let's leave the rest for now."*

- Blender itself failing: a scene too big for the card's memory, a crash part-way through a frame.
- A disk filling: the server's with frames mid-render, or the Mac's mid-download.
- The Mac sleeping, its lid closed, or its Wi-Fi changing mid-transfer (only a pressed *Disconnect* has been tried).
- Windows and Linux desktop apps never clicked through by a person.
- Two servers at once, or one server from two computers.

**Due:** when the Creator asks, and before the first sale at the latest.

Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` P4 and P5.*

P4 and P5 say every read and write of the local record runs off the thread that draws. Transfer piece 1 moved the two screens a transfer contends with — Transfers and Files — off it. **The other 48 commands that are not marked as running apart still take the application's lock on the thread that draws.** With a save now about 16 ms, nobody feels them, and moving them all touches every screen file.

**What it costs.** A short stall of the window if one of them meets a long save; nothing is lost.

**Due:** if the eleventh pass or the Creator sees the window freeze while pressing one of them; otherwise at step 12.

### A download into a very deep destination fails, where `Desktop app and delivery` C6 says it descends

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from the transfer map's huge-files list and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Huge files and huge folders at the desk — what was found.md`. Recorded as met and not built at `Audits/Build/Desktop app and delivery/Decisions record.md`, 2026-09-29. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` C6.*

C6 says the adapter's own writes descend rather than composing one long absolute path, so a fetched tree may go deeper than any absolute path macOS accepts. At the desk, a download into a destination already 895 bytes deep, so the landing path passed 1,024 bytes, ended *failed* and landed nothing: the platform file adapter opens by whole absolute paths (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/transfers.rs`, `src/platform/files.rs`).

**What it costs.** Nothing silent: the user is told *failed*. A destination that deep is not a real case.

**Due:** at step 12, or if a real user meets it; the fix reaches every local write in those two files.

### The pace of many small files on a real line, until transfer piece 13's ruling

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from the transfer map's carry-forward 6 and its plan pass (the conditional brief after piece 13), and the tenth-pass map's piece 5. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md` O1 and `Download` Q1.*

One small file costs about eight round trips, and uploads and manual downloads send one file at a time: `File Browser` O1's pool (four up) and `Download` Q1's ceiling (two down) are ceilings the code does not fill. So at 0.1–0.2 s a round trip, 10,000 small files take roughly two to four and a half hours on the Creator's line, whatever the record does. No plan promises a pace. Transfer piece 13 measures it at the desk; if round trips per file dominate, filling O1's and Q1's ceilings is the brief that follows, with no plan change.

**What it costs.** Hours for a huge folder. The Creator's huge-folders test is sized at about a thousand files until then (*The Creator's real-world tests*, above).

**Ruled, and met at the desk, 2026-09-30.** Transfer piece 13's ruling took filling the ceilings, and eleventh-pass fix piece 4 did it: at 0.2 s a round trip, 1,000 small files went up in 10.9 minutes (from 47.1) and came down in 11.7 (from 23.6) (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Eleventh-pass fix piece 4 — what landed.md`). **Not yet timed on a real line.** *Updated by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11).*

**Timed on the twelfth pass, but not alone, 2026-10-01.** Both 1,000-file timings ran beside a render's own downloads, which share the same pool of four, so they measure the pool and not the transfer (the fix map after the twelfth pass, carry-forward 5). *Updated by the paperwork piece of step 10c (twelfth-pass fix piece 8).*

~~**Due:** the thirteenth pass times 1,000 files up and down with nothing else moving on that server and the line measured at that moment; this item closes if the pace holds there.~~

**FIRED 2026-10-01: timed alone on the thirteenth pass, and the pace did not hold.** 1,000 small files went up in 15.6 minutes, 0.94 s a file, with nothing else moving and a round trip of 0.15–0.18 s; Transfers showed 2–3 files moving and 3 queued most of the time, though the pool allows four. Downloads came at 0.88 files a second (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The thirteenth pass — Hyperstack.md`). Why only 2–3 show as moving is not established; the time each file spends opening, closing and publishing, which is not drawn as moving, is the candidate. The fix map after the thirteenth pass weighed it a pace reading, not a fault. *Updated by the paperwork piece of step 10c (thirteenth-pass fix piece 4).*

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release. **A desk count of round trips per small file comes first**, the way twelfth-pass fix piece 3 counted the folder walk, before anything is cut.

### Two readings that grow with the record's history

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Transfer wave piece 1 — what landed.md` (*Found, and for the runner*) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Transfer wave piece 2 — what landed.md` (*For the runner*, item 3). Both notes went to the paperwork piece; neither code file is its to change. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` §5 and `Server protocol and transfers` S28.*

- **`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/the_underway_shape.rs`**, about line 192, reads every transfer record ever written, with its marks, through `transfer_records::all`. It is not on a transfer's path. It grows with the run's history, the way the admission's reading did before piece 1.
- **The start's sweep** (`src/start.rs`, since transfer piece 2) reads every download record ever ended *stopped*, *failed* or *stalled*, at every start, and costs one look at the disk per path. Records are never retired. It grows with given-up frames, not with published ones. Not measured.

**What it costs.** Nothing today; a slower screen or a slower start after long use.

**Due:** if transfer piece 13 or a later measurement finds either slow; otherwise at step 12, beside *Transfers lists every upload request ever kept*.

### The rail's figure lagging a job's end while a setup runs

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the tenth pass — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, item 4b. A watch, not a fault.*

On the tenth pass the rail kept *1* for about a minute after the job ended. The rail moves when the read-forward learns the ending; a setup run was going at that moment, and the supervisor's asks take turns, which slows the read-forward. The figure was true to what Smoozy had learned.

### A dependency reading already asked for cannot be cancelled

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The dependency wave — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md` (pieces 5 and 11) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Dependency wave piece 5 — what landed.md`. Met, and not built. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` §3.4 and `Render Settings` W1.*

`Render Settings` W1 declares the file-inspection reading cancellable at any moment, and `Dependency Upload & Remap` §3.4's loop names cancelling the one in flight. **Nothing sends that cancel.** A stop during *Check dependencies…* ends the sitting *stopped by the user*, and a reading already asked of the machine runs on to its end and is thrown away when it lands. S1 recorded the same: a stop naming a file-inspection reading is refused before it is recorded.

**What it costs.** A reading that runs on after the user stopped, on a machine they are paying for; nothing wrong is stored.

**Due:** with the next piece that builds a stop for non-render work, and at step 12 at the latest.

### The phone's stop reaches nothing

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/S3 — what landed.md` (*For the runner*, item 5) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/S3's loose ends — what landed.md`, ruled by the build runner that day. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` and `Rented-server runtime`'s Telegram role worker.*

The queue's own *Stop* and *Cancel* now act from the desktop. **A stop sent from Telegram does not**, because the machine program's Telegram role worker is not built, so nothing on the machine reads the phone's command. **Ruled not for 0.1.7**, a stated gap.

**What it costs.** A user who presses stop on the phone stops nothing.

**Due:** with the piece that builds the Telegram role worker; before any release claims the phone can stop a render.

### Where a path can still read out of order

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from `Audits/Build/Desktop app and delivery/Decisions record.md`, the entries for dependency pieces 10a and 10b, both ruled by the build runner as stated gaps. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` X9 and `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md`.*

Every name and path on the screens now reads in the order its bytes run, through one shared drawing. **Four places cannot**, because `Desktop app and delivery` X9 forbids putting a character into the value:
- **a server's name in Transfers' server filter**, and the Blender version and Telegram lists on Render Settings — a list entry is drawn by the system from a plain string;
- **the machine's own words** (`web/the-machines-own-words.js`), which stay one text node, as its M9 comment requires: a block of many lines, so a path inside one line can still read out of order;
- a tooltip or a spoken label;
- a value typed into a field, which is the user's own.

**What it costs.** A name with right-to-left letters may read in the wrong order in those places only.

**Due:** at step 13, the words swept; or at the next Design sitting that opens `Whole app`.

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from the build runner's list in `Audits/Build/Progress.md` (the block *2026-09-29, THE SITTING THAT OPENS TRANSFER WAVE 2*, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The removal's stop and clear — what it is and how big.md`) and the runner's ruling on dependency piece 7b.*

But `Server Setup`'s removal is still owed, `Queue & History` L21's mark has no writer, and **it blocks the watch *a server with no Blender***: until it is built, a pass makes that fixture by moving the Blender aside by hand. Not before 0.1.7 unless the build runner rules it in.

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the eleventh pass — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, section 1 item 5, and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Eleventh-pass fix pieces 1 and 5 — what landed.md`. On the eleventh pass a fresh copy's first download waited on macOS's own box asking whether Smoozy may reach the Downloads folder. **No plan mentions that permission at all.** Piece 1 made the request read under way, never *done*, while the box stands. **What the user reads if they press *Don't Allow* is unchecked** — most likely a failed file with an unclassified reason.

**What it costs.** Possibly a failure that does not say why. Nothing is lost.

### Words from the eleventh pass, for step 13

Both are true — the send changed the row — but the second reads as though the file changed, and *Already on the server* is said twice on one row (`Design/Dependency Upload & Remap`, the mockup's `.cstay` row).
- **The box after a cut setup** says the connection dropped *at* the run's start: `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/set_up.rs`, near line 3449, dates it with the last boundary reached, not the drop, which nothing records. The words are Design's.
- ***Frame N is rendered and downloaded.*** on a held first-frame check. *Downloaded* is Design's word and is not checked: it is true only where frames come down as they render.

### A held job's answer in hand while the line is cut reads *Not updating* alone

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Eleventh-pass fix piece 9 — what landed.md`, section 7, ruled a stated gap by the build runner that day. Tagged for `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md` and `Queue & History`.*

Piece 9 keeps a stop or a cancel said beside *Not updating* when the line goes quiet. **A held job's *Render the rest* or *Cancel it* in hand at that moment is not**: the entry reads *Not updating* and nothing about the answer. It is one exchange, not a waiter, and nothing false is said.

**What it costs.** For up to two minutes the user is not told their answer is still being sent.

**Due:** if the twelfth pass or the Creator meets it; otherwise at step 12.

### Two gaps on the phone's route for a held first-frame check

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Eleventh-pass fix piece 8b — what landed.md`, section 8 items 2 and 3, both ruled stated gaps by the build runner that day. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Rented-server runtime.md` U12a and S14, and `Telegram`.*

- **A *yes* at the desk that races a *no* from the phone is never told the *no* won.** The *no* removes the job with its hold record, so the *yes* is answered *no such hold* until the desk's two-minute waiter gives up. The next reading drops the job, so nothing false is shown, but the user is not told why. Telling it would need the desk to read the machine's own word for this answer, which piece 8 decided not to.
- **A *no* whose release the machine's store held pending** (S14) removes the job only when that *no* is asked again. The desk asks again; the phone's route has no second ask.

**What it costs.** A silent end to a *yes*; a held job that stays until the next *no* or *Cancel*.

**Due:** with the piece that builds the Telegram role worker, beside *The phone's stop reaches nothing*.

### On a machine that allows one session, a download waits behind up to four upload files

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Eleventh-pass fix piece 4 — what landed.md` (*Existing tests brought to four at once*, and *Met, and not built* item 5), ruled accepted by the build runner that day. A note, not a fault. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md` O5.*

Since piece 4 an upload request moves four files at once. **At a machine that allows only one session**, a download beside such an upload is seated after at most four of its files, where it was after one. That is inside `File Browser` O5's share, but the four channels buy nothing there, and the server's point orders no transfers (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/servers/sessions.rs`), so O5's pool is not built at that point.

**What it costs.** A slower download beside a big upload, on a machine none of the four providers has shown.

**Due:** only if a one-session machine is met on a pass or by a user.

### `File Browser` N8 for an upload made before a server's first setup

*Added 2026-10-01 by the plan pass before the fixes after step 10c's twelfth pass, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the twelfth pass — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md` (carry-forward 3, and *Not written now* in its plan pass), parked on the build runner's ruling. A real choice, not a hole. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md` N6 and N8.*

An upload entry counts under the installation in force (`File Browser` N6, N8), and an upload made before a server's first setup had none. So once the server is set up, Smoozy has no record of where that file came from on the user's computer, and a dependency check on it runs `Dependency Upload & Remap` K5's reduced form. Design's line at the top of the check now says so (`Preparation/Design/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` § *Above the rows*, added the same day).

**The case.** The twelfth pass (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The twelfth pass — Hyperstack.md`, fault 8): the 4.5 battle `.blend` was uploaded at 21:15 to a re-added server with no installation; *Set up this server* gave it one at 21:21; the check at 21:39 found none of 58.

**What it costs.** A file uploaded before *Set up this server* gets the reduced check. The Creator's route is *Add and set up*, so it is met only on purpose, and uploading the file again after setup gives the full check.

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release, or sooner if a user meets it.

### On Windows, the dependency check's folder search could record a location the plan gives no shape on the server

*Added 2026-10-01 by the plan pass before the fixes after step 10c's twelfth pass, from the build runner's ruling on the board (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`, 2026-09-30, the hosted run's second fix), under the Creator's standing delegation. A stated gap. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap.md` A4 and §12 item 3.*

On Windows the check's search of the user's folders can still find a file and record where it was. Where that file would land on the server is what the plan leaves open: A4's mirror shape for a drive-lettered location is undecided, inside §12 item 3's Windows halves. The five `checking_a_file.rs` tests that find a `.blend`'s file on the user's computer run on macOS and Linux only, with that reason written in them.

**This is not a pending answer.** §12 item 3 stays permanently unresolved as written, and nothing here schedules a Windows computer or leaves a space for one.

**What it costs.** On Windows, a found file could be sent to a place on the server whose shape nobody chose. It does not block 0.1.7's Mac passes.

**Due:** before the first Windows release carries the dependency check. It goes on the list told to the Creator before that release.

### A download cut by a dropped line starts over, though the reading it needs is taken on every rental

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (twelfth-pass fix piece 8), from the fix map after the twelfth pass (carry-forward 2, fault 2, and section 8). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` S13 and `Download` M11a; no plan change is expected.*

A download taken up after a cut or a *Disconnect* starts the file again. `Server protocol and transfers` S13's four-value reading of the file on the server has no caller when a download is arranged: `the_four_value_reading_taken_now` (`App/Server protocol and transfers/src/transfers/moving.rs`) answers nothing, so S13's *could not be confirmed* branch starts the file over and says so. The transfer map accepted this, and the Creator is told before their tests that a download cut by a dropped line starts that file again.

**A correction the code still owes, written here rather than in the file.** `App/Download/src/arriving_later.rs` calls the reading *permanently unresolved under the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31*, on the ground that what program answers on an arbitrary rental was never established. **That is not true.** The same reading is taken on every rental today, at submission (`App/Render Settings/src/acting/four_values.rs`, `read_the_four_values`) and by the dependency check (`App/Dependency Upload & Remap/src/acting/machine.rs`, `read_the_four_values`). What is missing is a caller at the moment a download is arranged, and nothing else. The comment above `the_four_value_reading_taken_now` gives the same wrong reason, and `App/Download/src/acting/fetching.rs` (`TheFourValues`) still says the reading has no producer anywhere in the workspace. Whoever builds the caller corrects all three.

**What it costs.** A large download cut part-way fetches again from the start. Nothing false is said.

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release, for files large enough to have a checkpoint.

**CLOSED 2026-10-04** by the 38's piece 9 (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 9 — what landed.md`, item 18; the account committed at `c9239592`, the code built and not yet committed): a manual download's file larger than one checkpoint interval has S13's four values read when it is arranged and again before it carries on, so it carries on where the file on the server has not changed, and starts over saying so where it has. `the_four_value_reading_taken_now` is gone, and the three wrong sentences this entry names were corrected. An auto-download's cut fetch still starts over, by `Download` P3. Watched at the next pass (that account's section 9, items 1 and 2). What the piece leaves is parked below (*The 38's piece 9: where a carried-on download, a leftover's Remove and Which ones fall short*). *Noted 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the test copy's notes pieces 5, 7 and 6, and 8, the 38's pieces 7, 9 and 15, and the two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 in its place by the build runner.*

### The drain's pace on a finished job, once the thirteenth pass has counted the channels

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (twelfth-pass fix piece 8), from the fix map after the twelfth pass (carry-forward 5, fault 11, and section 8). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` T1 and `Download` Q1. It takes over the trigger of *Piece 3 of the fixes after the eleventh pass was not taken*, above.*

On the twelfth pass Smoozy drained a finished job's frames at 0.83 frames a second, the same as one `sftp` connection (0.81); two `sftp` connections did 1.53, and the desk's model for two channels at that distance is about 1.2 to 1.4. **Whether Smoozy had two channels open is not in the record**, so the cause is not established. The thirteenth pass counts the machine's `sftp-server` processes during Smoozy's drain, with nothing else moving on that server (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the thirteenth pass, through the shortcut.md`, *What binds*, item 3).

**What it costs.** A finished job's frames come down at about half the pace two connections could give.

~~**Due:** when the thirteenth pass's count is read. If two channels were open and the pace still matches one connection, the eleventh-pass map's piece 3 and its plan sentence E0-3 are briefed after 0.1.7's release; if one was open, why the second did not open is found first.~~

**FIRED 2026-10-01: counted on the thirteenth pass, and it is the first branch.** Smoozy had two channels open beside its reading channel (three `sftp-server` processes in 26 of 41 reads) and drained 0.82 frames a second, with nothing else moving; one `sftp` connection moved 0.81 and two moved 1.47 (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The thirteenth pass — Hyperstack.md`). **So two channels on one connection move what one connection moves.** Why is not established by reading. The fix map after the thirteenth pass weighed it a pace reading, not a fault. *Updated by the paperwork piece of step 10c (thirteenth-pass fix piece 4).*

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release, when the eleventh-pass map's piece 3 and its plan sentence E0-3 (a second connection for the drain) are briefed.

### A stalled upload's part-sent file is never cleared

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (twelfth-pass fix piece 8), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Twelfth-pass fix piece 1 — what landed.md` (its short answer, *The section's desk items*, and *Found on the way*). Left owed by the build runner (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`, the block beginning *PIECE 1 IS BUILT*). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` C19, S26 and S27.*

Since piece 1, an upload whose machine stops answering on a connection that stays up ends *stalled* at C19's two minutes. *Stalled* removes the staged part (S26), but that removal would be one more wait on the channel that has just gone unanswered, so it is not asked: the record is marked as owing the clean-up (S27, `cleanup_is_owed`, in `App/Server protocol and transfers/src/transfers/moving.rs`). **Nothing settles that debt on the server**, and the leftover row reads *part-sent files are still using space on the server* with no *Remove*, since `the_leftovers_are_told` offers *Remove* only for *interrupted* records. A cut line that comes back never reaches *stalled*: it ends *interrupted* and carries on.

**Found beside it, in the same piece.** A few waits outside the byte loop still have no deadline: in `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/uploading.rs`, the staging folder asked for in `one_file_goes_up` and the subsystem negotiated in `a_slot_and_a_seat`; in `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/the_found_files_sent.rs`, the mirror's folders, the staging folder and the landing's reading. A silent cut now ends them at the break. A machine that stops answering on a live connection still holds them with no end.

**What it costs.** A part-sent file takes space on the server until the user removes it themselves. Both cases need a machine that stops answering while its connection stays up, which no pass has met.

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release, or sooner if a pass reads a *stalled* row.

### Counting a folder for download costs four round trips a folder

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (twelfth-pass fix piece 8), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Twelfth-pass fix piece 3 — what landed.md` (*The count, timed before anything was cut*). Tagged for `Download`'s walk (`App/Download/src/acting/`) and `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` §1.1 item 2.*

At the desk, at a round trip of 0.15 s, 1,000 files in 45 folders count in 28.2 s alone and 31.0 s beside a drain. Each folder costs four round trips, one after another: open, read, read again, close. **Nothing was cut.** Two speed-ups are named and not made:
1. **The folder's close need not be waited for.** That is a quarter of the count.
2. **The count's readings are asked as ones nobody is waiting on** (`TheWalksReadingRoute`, `someone_is_waiting = false`), though `a_download_is_prepared`'s own comment calls the walk a reading the user waits on.

**The twelfth pass's three minutes were not reproduced.** The server's point serves commands and seats before readings (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/servers/sessions.rs`), so on a busy period each folder reading waits. The thirteenth pass times the count alone and beside a render.

**Timed alone on the thirteenth pass, 2026-10-01: 74 seconds**, with nothing else moving and the line measured at that moment (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The thirteenth pass — Hyperstack.md`). At that pass's median round trip of 0.179 s, four round trips a folder predicts about 33 s, so the pass took about twice that, and **the cause is not established**. The fix map after the thirteenth pass weighed it a pace reading, not a fault, and keeps the two speed-ups above as the cuts to make. *Updated by the paperwork piece of step 10c (thirteenth-pass fix piece 4).*

**What it costs.** About a minute before a 1,000-file download starts at that distance, more on a busy server. The row reads *Counting files…* with *Stop* meanwhile, so nothing is silent.

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release, with the two speed-ups above, and why the pass took twice the desk's figure found first.

### A failed downloaded file has no *Try again* of its own

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (thirteenth-pass fix piece 4), from the fix map after the thirteenth pass (piece 3) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Thirteenth-pass fix piece 3 — what landed.md`. Owed by the plan, held by no piece. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Download.md` P3 and `Preparation/Design/Transfers.md` § *A file that failed*.*

`Download` P3 says a manual download's failed file stays failed, with its own *Try again* as the user's act. Since thirteenth-pass fix piece 3 such a file has its own row, reading *Download failed.* with *Details*, and **no *Try again***, because the control is unbuilt for every download (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/transfers.rs`, *Still not built*) and a button that does nothing would be false.

**What it costs.** To try one failed file again, the user starts its download again.

**Due:** before the Build stage closes, with *Acts that still answer* not built, above.

**CLOSED 2026-10-04** by the 38's piece 9 (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 9 — what landed.md`, item 17; the account committed at `c9239592`, the code built and not yet committed): a failed download's row carries its own *Try again* first after its sentence, which starts a new request for that one file into the folder it was going to. *Noted 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the test copy's notes pieces 5, 7 and 6, and 8, the 38's pieces 7, 9 and 15, and the two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 in its place by the build runner.*

### The download box counts the file's range, not a typed one

*Added 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c after the Sesterce pass (Sesterce-pass fix piece 3), from the fix map after the Sesterce pass (*Smaller*) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Sesterce-pass fix piece 1 — what landed.md` (*For the runner*, 3). ### Two provider readings from the Vast pass that Smoozy's own guidance can lead a user into

*Added 2026-10-01 by the the paperwork piece of step 10c after the Vast pass, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Vast pass — the Creator's real-world tests.md` (*Provider readings*, 1 and 2). Label: **observed in a test**, one day, one provider. Tagged for `Preparation/Specs/Whole app.md` (the step it hands the user) and the server form's lead, `Rent a Linux server — an Ubuntu or Debian one.` (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/servers.rs`, `STEP_4_LEAD`).*

- **Vast's `ubuntu:24.04` image never took a key**: four instances in two countries, keys attached per instance and stored on the account, one restart. `ubuntu:22.04` on the same host took it at once. **Smoozy's *Rent a Linux server — an Ubuntu or Debian one* would lead a user straight into it**, and they could not log in.
- **`download.blender.org` refused one Vast host (Florida) with a 403 and a Cloudflare challenge.** Smoozy's setup said, truthfully, *The server could not fetch the Blender build.* On such a host the only way to a Blender is *choose a build on your computer*, which is not built (*Acts that still answer not built*, above: *Point at a build on this computer*).

**What it costs.** A user who follows Smoozy's guidance onto either can be left with a server Smoozy cannot reach or cannot give a Blender. Both are the provider's, not Smoozy's, but the guidance and the setup's words are Smoozy's.

**Due:** before the first public release.

**Due:** only if a user's report needs to know where a download was refused.

### A real sleep of the Mac, after the faked one

*Added 2026-10-01 by the the paperwork piece of step 10c after the Vast pass, from the build runner's ruling on the board (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`, the block *THE CREATOR, 2026-10-01, two rulings*) and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the check before 0.1.7, with a faked sleep.md`, watch 4.*

The check before 0.1.7 fakes a closed lid: it freezes Smoozy's process and cuts the server's line for about twenty minutes, then restores both. **The stated difference:** on a real sleep the Mac's timer clock stops while wall time jumps; a frozen process sees both run. So a fault that hangs on that difference would not show in the fake.

**What it costs.** One kind of fault on waking that no test has looked for.

**Due:** after 0.1.7's release: a real lid-closed test, with a render and a download running.

*Added 2026-10-02 by the build runner (Claude Opus 5.5), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The release pass on 0.1.7 — Vast.md`.* After *Stop* cancels the frame, Blender steps through every remaining frame of the range without rendering, then quits — about two minutes for 600 frames, just past Smoozy's wait, so Queue asked for a second press 8 seconds before Blender ended by itself. Nothing is lost and nothing false is said. **Seen again on RunPod the same day** (`The release pass on 0.1.7 — RunPod.md`): there Blender quit 1 min 52 s after the press, inside the wait, so one press was enough — whether a person meets the second press comes down to about ten seconds of machine speed. **What is owed**: the wait allows for that walk, or the stop ends it.

### Two faults the check before 0.1.7 left, not release-stopping (2026-10-02)

*Added 2026-10-02 by the build runner (Claude Opus 5.5), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The check before 0.1.7 — Hyperstack.md`, New faults 2 and 3. Ruled not to stop 0.1.7 (the board, 2026-10-02).*

1. **A transfer under way while Smoozy is frozen ends *stalled*, reads only *— did not finish* with no reason, and drops what it fetched** (958 MB of a 3 GB download). Seen on both faked sleeps. A real sleep stops the timer clock Smoozy's waits use, so it may not happen there; a line silent past two minutes with the connection kept would. **What is owed**: the row says why it stopped. A download starting the file again is already on the list the Creator is told before their tests.
2. **A stalled upload's part is left on the server with no *Remove*** — the row says *part-sent files are still using space on the server* and offers nothing (501 MB left in `~/.smoozy-staging`); the next upload did not use it.

**Due:** after 0.1.7 is released, with the fixes after its passes.

### While Smoozy's program copies a file, that server's other requests wait (2026-10-02)

*Added 2026-10-02 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Files' owed acts piece 7 — what landed.md`, section 6. Ruled by the build runner a stated gap, not a fault (`Audits/Build/Desktop app and delivery/Decisions record.md`, 2026-10-02). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` §1.1's paragraph of 2026-10-02 and `File Browser` M7.*

Where the server's file service cannot copy, Smoozy's own program on the machine copies each file. The desktop sends that program one request at a time, and a copy is one of them, so while a long copy runs everything else asked of that server's program waits its turn. **Expected** from the code, not measured: on a 4 GB copy, Queue's readings of that server pause for the copy's length. Nothing false is said and nothing is lost.

**What it costs.** Queue on that server stops moving while the copy runs.

**Due:** the next rental pass measures it — piece 7's watch list copies a 4 GB `.blend` on a plain Ubuntu 22.04 image and watches Queue on that server meanwhile.

### Uploaded files land on the server readable only by their owner (2026-10-02)

*Added 2026-10-02 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after the checks' leftovers.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/After 0.1.8, the checks' leftovers — what landed.md`, item 5. Left on the build runner's ruling: not a fault against the plans, which are silent on an uploaded file's mode.*

A file Smoozy uploads lands with mode 0600 — its owner can read and write it, nobody else can. Blender runs as the same login, so nothing breaks today.

**What it costs.** Nothing yet. Another login on the same server, or a tool run as another user, could not read an uploaded file.

**Due:** only if a job, or the Creator, needs a second login on one server to read what Smoozy uploaded. It would enter at Architecture (`Server protocol and transfers`).

### A cache folder sent through the check goes at about 1.3 s a file (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after the Vast fixes' rulings.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/After the Vast fixes, the whole desk, four rulings and the slow send — what landed.md`, part 3, and the build runner's ruling of 2026-10-03 (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Server protocol and transfers.md` §1.1 and F2, and `Dependency Upload & Remap` Q5.*

On the Vast pass, a cache folder of small files sent through *Check dependencies* went at about 1.3 s a file, against 0.36 s for a plain `sftp` of the same folder. **Measured at the desk, in a copy, not fixed.** Every file queues at the one link site per server: its own transfer session's opening, and the four-value reading, which takes four requests at the link and a command. About eight round trips a file, one after another.

The builder's line, word for word: *2026-10-03, measured in a copy, not fixed — the cause is the plans' own shape; the three ways out and the one in-plan trim are in this account's part 3.*

**RULED by the build runner, 2026-10-03: the first of the account's three ways out.** Read the four values through Smoozy's own installed program, as copy already goes (Files' owed acts piece 7), not through the link site — the route `Server protocol and transfers` F2 route 2 names after hand-over. One new request, on both sides, and a server release. **Built in a later sitting, with its plan line written first.** The other two ways out (several link sites, or several files on one transfer channel) change `Server protocol and transfers` §1.1 and T1 and were not taken.

**Also left, and not built:** the account's one trim inside the plans — `plant_the_carriage` (`App/Server Setup/src/acts.rs`) plants first and makes the folder and removes an old link only when that fails, saving 2 of the reading's 4 requests. It shares a function with the placement and the version ask, which real-machine passes debugged, so it wants its own piece and pass. ~~**The ruled route removes the link from the reading anyway**, so it matters only if that route is not built.~~ **Corrected 2026-10-03 by the build runner: it does not. Every ask to Smoozy's own program plants and removes its own link too, so only gathering many files into one ask saves anything, and this trim now speeds every such ask as well.** **The plan line is written** (2026-10-03): one request reading the four values of many files at once, in `Server protocol and transfers` §1.1 and F2 and `Dependency Upload & Remap` Q5 — `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The plan pass for the slow send, a Servers sentence and C6 — what was done.md`. The measuring count (`measure_a_cache_folder_of_48_small_files_sent`, widened) is not in the tree: it lives only in the builder's scratch folder named in the account (`measure.patch` and the copy `bite/`), which a temporary folder may not keep, so the later sitting may have to write it again to compare before and after. Beside *The pace of many small files on a real line*, above, which is the plain upload's pace and not this route.

**What it costs.** A cache folder of a few thousand files takes about an hour through the check where `sftp` would take about twenty minutes. Nothing false is said.

**Due:** before the release after 0.1.9, or sooner if the Creator meets it. Watch 6 of the account times it on a real line, with the line measured at the same moment.

**Built at the desk, 2026-11-06, under the runner's bake-folder brief.** The 2026-11-04 change gathered the check and pre-flight only; publication and folder *Confirm* remained one file at a time, and the Linux pass measured 500 files in 10 min 47 s. Both remaining paths use the existing many-file request, with the older-program route retained. Publication groups are bounded at 64 per channel; libraries and large files flush immediately. No protocol version moves. The implementation, before-and-after desk measurements and test results are in `Audits/Build/Blender 5.3/A bake folder's send, and the stale reading words — what landed.md`. The unbuilt work is closed; the real-line timing remains owed, and the separate `plant_the_carriage` trim above was not changed.

**Standing:** the batching work is built; the 1.3 s/file and 10 min 47 s figures describe earlier code. **Due:** a real-line before-and-after timing at the film's file count before release, with the line measured beside it. This sitting rented no machine.

### The machine's plan total assumes a 2048 tile size (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after piece 6a and the 38's piece 1.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's notes on 0.1.8, piece 6a — what landed.md`, sections 2 and 6, and the build runner's ruling of 2026-10-03 (`Audits/Build/Progress.md`). Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Queue & History.md` B2 and the reading script.*

The machine works out `Queue & History` B1's planned total once per render (`the_plan_total_of`, in `App/Rendering — machine/src/acting/pipeline.rs`). B2 counts tiles over the file's own tile size, but the reading never reads it, so the total assumes 2048, the default B2 records. It is labelled an assumption in the code and in `Audits/Build/Rendering/Decisions record.md`. **Ruled by the build runner, 2026-10-03: it stays assumed for now.** Reading it means naming a Blender setting, which needs a source first (Rule 2), and then a change to the reading script.

**What it costs.** A file with another tile size gets a planned total off by the tile factor. Every frame of a job has one output size, so every renderlet is off by the same factor and the second bar's fraction does not move. Only the plan figure is wrong.

**Due:** when the reading script is next opened, or sooner if a pass shows a wrong total.

### The 38's piece 1: what the notifications leave unsaid, and what was not compiled or tested (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after piece 6a and the 38's piece 1.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 1 — what landed.md` (*Not done, and why*; *Where the record and the world stop agreeing*; *Rulings I made*, item 5; *Also touched*; and its record lines). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: an ending that lands while the line is cut (`Desktop app and delivery` §22 item 26, by design); nothing ever really posted on any system (§22 item 11); the notification's picture (the map's piece 3); the pass on all four providers (the board).*

**Not compiled or not tested at this desk:**
- **The Linux and Windows notification files** (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/notifications/linux.rs` and `windows.rs`, one line each) were never compiled here. The builder matched their existing patterns. The next hosted run is their first check.
- **P10d's pass posting an ending, and the idle re-check's own thread,** are not tested end to end; each needs a running app. Every part under them is tested. The account's watches 2 to 8 cover them on the next pass.

**Left in the code and the plans:**
- **`App/Queue & History/src/arriving_later.rs`** still says `AsksAMachineForItsQueue` has no implementation, though the desktop implements it. The builder corrected the rest of that sentence and flagged this part. Tagged for that file.
- **`Servers` D4 names no idle-spoken mark**, though the removal walk now takes them (`KeyedByTheServer::IdleSpokenMarks`). A plan fault, recorded in `Audits/Build/Servers/Decisions record.md` and not repaired from Build. D4 took a sentence for the told-records the same way on 2026-09-14. The wider question about the local record's inventory, which already names this mark, is the item above headed *The local record has a record kind nobody's inventory names*. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Servers.md` D4.
- **Design's ending notifications show figures no record holds**: a frame total (*240 frames*, *of 240*) and *after two interruptions*. The built second line leaves both out. Under Rule 10 the Design text is what changes. *Less than a minute* is Build's own wording, for step 13. Tagged for `Preparation/Design/Whole app.md` § Notifications.

**Where a notification is missing, or says something untrue:**
- **An ending put out in the last few seconds before a connected period ends is never posted**, because P10d's next pass never comes. History still shows the ending.
- **A live ending whose note could not be read is never posted.** It is learned later, without a notification.
- **The idle mark is written before the post, and a post is never confirmed** (`Desktop app and delivery` T23). If the system drops the post, Smoozy has marked as told something the user never saw.
- **A server running 0.1.8 or older gives no now, so it is never told about as idle** until it runs the new program.
- **A machine installing Blender for half an hour with an empty line is told it has nothing to do.** `Queue & History` W1, as written, counts only renders as work. This one says something untrue.
- **A group with a member whose start was never heard never finishes**, so its batch notification never comes.
- **Each re-check reads the whole retained journal again**, though only on a nudge or at its scheduled time.

**What it costs.** Two systems' notification code is unchecked until the hosted run. Two plan texts say less, or more, than the app does. A few endings and idle stretches are never told, and one case tells the user a busy machine has nothing to do. History still holds every ending.

**Due:** the Linux and Windows files at the next hosted run; the two threads at the next pass that runs the desktop app connected; the `arriving_later.rs` sentence with the next piece that touches that file; `Servers` D4 and the Design sentence at the runner's next plan pass; the installing case, for the runner to weigh under Rule 8, before the release that carries the idle notification; the rest if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### The 0.1.8 notes' pieces 6b and 6c: what the live figures and the live words leave (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's notes on 0.1.8, pieces 6b and 6c — what landed.md` (section 2, its readings and *Not done, on purpose*; section 6). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: no figures across a supervisor restart (`Queue & History` R5); the whole-job bar moving only when a frame is saved (B6's new paragraph); the first bar on 5.x with several view layers (`Queue & History` §18 item 28); the rental pass and its watches, and the bare markers the desk's guard flagged (both on the board).*

**Left in the code and the plans:**
- **The rendering entry's newest-line span is not drawn.** The drawing has one. The builder read `Desktop app and delivery` M13b as winning over it, as the page's head and `src/presentation` record. If M13b covers only a failure, this is a one-line Design question, not a fault. Tagged for the runner, then `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md`, a rendering entry.
- **`TheActOnAJob::answers` still answers *not built* for *Blender output*** (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/the_queue.rs`), its comment says History's entry reaches it, and `every_act_on_a_job_either_acts_or_is_named_here_as_owed` still lists it as owed. 6c left it for History's press. 6d then gave History its own ask (`TheHistoryAsk::BlenderOutput`), so the arm looks unreachable now. Nobody has checked that. Tagged for that file.
- **The entry's machine readings (card memory, free disk) stay empty.** `entry.readings` is `Queue & History` G5a's reading at entry open, not P10d's and not this piece's, though the notes' map expected it filled. Nothing builds it. Tagged for `Queue & History` G5a.

**Where the screen and the machine stop agreeing:**
- **A worker that dies without an ending**: its last figures are drawn for one more pass at most, and on the first reading after a connection a dead worker's figures are drawn once, because nothing earlier exists to compare against. - **The panel after the job ends** keeps what it had and receives nothing more. The words then belong to History.

**What it costs.** A few seconds of a dead worker's figures, live words that do not say what was dropped, and the unbuilt or stale bits above. Nothing is lost: History keeps the whole words.

**Due:** the newest-line question at the runner's next plan pass; the `answers` arm with the next piece that touches `the_queue.rs`; the entry's readings and R12's mark when the runner next maps what the plans promise and nothing builds; the rest if a pass or the Creator meets one.
