### The 0.1.8 notes' piece 6d: words that are never fetched, and one route left empty (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's notes on 0.1.8, piece 6d — what landed.md` (section 7, and section 6's rulings 2 and 3). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the words' size in the local record, about 5 kB a frame and kept until the entry is deleted (`Queue & History` E9's cost note); the rental pass and its watches, and the desk's one failing marker guard, which is 6b's (both on the board).*

**Where the record and the machine stop agreeing:**
- **A debt nothing will clear.** A job whose fetch was cut and whose machine was then destroyed keeps `words_owed` for good, since that server is never connected again. The entry offers no *Blender output* (`Queue & History` E9's boundary). The flag stays in the record.
- **Rentals still on 0.1.8** send no capture records. Their jobs keep their debt and get no words, at one note reading per job per connection, until the server runs the new program.
- **Jobs that ended before record version 34** never have their words fetched, even where the machine still has them.
- **One large read holds the reading session.** A sink is read in one turn, marked "nobody is waiting". A Files listing asked meanwhile waits for the read in hand: on a slow line, a 50 MB stream could hold it for some seconds, once, right after a long job's ending is learned. *Expected*, not measured.

**Left in the code:**
- **`the_machines_own_words_for`** in `App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/the_machines_own_words.rs`, the failure record's route, still composes nothing, and the file's head says what supplies it is *Nothing*. History's panel comes through the history event instead, so the route is unused rather than wrong. Tagged for that file.

**What it costs.** Some jobs never offer *Blender output*: those run on an older program, those ended before version 34, and those whose machine went before the fetch finished. Smoozy says nothing false about them; it offers no control. One stray flag per such job stays in the record.

**Due:** the slow read at the next pass that times a long job's fetch (the account's watch 6), measured against the line at that moment; the 0.1.8 case closes itself as servers take the new program; the stray debt and the unused route with the next piece that touches the record or that file; the older jobs' words only if the Creator asks for them.

### The 38's piece 10: jobs whose reading never reaches the desktop (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 10 and 5 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 10 — what landed.md` (section 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: which scene a file saved from Blender's interface shows (`Rendering` L16, its §9 item 12, and the account's watch 5 on the board's next pass); figures and hold acts answered from a phone (unchanged, `Rented-server runtime` U13–U15); the rental pass on all four providers (the board). Left out because it is not a gap: after Smoozy restarts, the six statements come back at the first connection's step 7, and the queue is blank until then anyway.*

**Where the record and the machine stop agreeing:**
- **A reading missed in the moment its job ended.** When the reading cannot be read as its event comes (a hiccup on the reading session), the event is passed anyway. A job still in the queue is read again at the next connection. A job that ended in that moment is never read again, so its History row shows the dash.
- **Jobs that ended under a desktop older than this piece** have no reading and no asked order on their record, and nothing reads one for them: the fill-in covers only jobs still in the queue. Their rows keep the dash.
- **A held job on a machine program older than this piece** still carries the old reason, so its entry offers *Cancel* only, as before.

**What it costs.** Some History rows show the dash where a count could have been drawn, and a hold on an old program offers no *Carry on anyway*. Nothing false is said.

**Due:** the old-program hold closes itself as servers take the new program; the missed reading and the older jobs only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### The 38's piece 5: what a selling build still lacks, two plan seams, and two cases where the licence and the record part (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 10 and 5 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 5 — what landed.md` (both *Lines for the decisions record*, *Where the record and the world stop agreeing*, and *The desk*). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the service's home and the seller account (`Audits/Build/The sweep of 2026-09-07/What waits on the Creator.md`, items 1 and 2, and the map's piece 5); the watch on the Creator's real service (the map's piece 5); old test programs for four packages in `App/target-a/` (the board).*

**What a selling build still lacks:**
- **The licence recovery root, which nobody holds.** `smoozy_access::request::THIS_BUILD` holds four things a selling release must fill, all absent: the service's address, the *buy* page, the *recover* page and the recovery root. The first three wait on the service and the seller account above. **The fourth is the Creator's offline licence key, and it is on no list of what waits on the Creator.** Without it nothing the service sends can be verified. `Preparation/Architecture/What Build inherits.md` names this root as a single point of failure; it does not say none exists yet. Tagged for `What waits on the Creator.md` and the board, beside the rule that no release selling a key goes out before piece 5 answers the real service.

**Two plan seams, built ahead of their sentences:**
- **`Desktop app and delivery` P10 allows exactly one timer, the update check; `Access` C1's cadence is a second.** Built per C1. P10 calls any second timer a fault in P10, so P10 needs a line admitting C1's. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Desktop app and delivery.md` P10.
- **`Desktop app and delivery` K25 names "the shell's own browser-opening call"; Tauri's core has none** (it is a plugin Smoozy does not hold). Built as platform code beside `reveal.rs`. K25's route needs correcting to what was built. Tagged for K25.

**Not built at this desk:**
- **The address opener's Windows and Linux branches** (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/the_address_opener.rs`) have never been compiled on the Mac. The next hosted run is their first look.

**Where the licence and the record part:**
- **A release the service confirmed but whose local lock would not write** leaves the service with the slot freed and this computer still holding the key. Its next check takes a slot again, so the computer the user meant to free a slot for can be refused.
- **A check answered while the user typed a new key** is discarded and run again.

**What it costs.** Nothing until selling starts: a desk-built copy reaches no address and runs the trial. Once selling, a missing recovery root means no key can be confirmed at all, and the slot case can cost a user one more release.

**Due:** the recovery root before any release that sells a key, with the service and the seller account; the two plan seams at the runner's next plan pass; the opener's two branches at the next hosted run; the two cases if a pass or the Creator meets one.


Superseded by the Creator’s decision of 2026-10-04; recorded 2026-10-26. Paid-layer work is withdrawn. Earlier rulings and closures remain as history. This includes the licence recovery root, paid record cases and P10’s licence-check exception. The ordinary address opener, its K25 wording and its Windows/Linux checks remain owed on their existing terms.

### The 38's piece 2: a plan seam, a mark not drawn, and what is held only for the run (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 2 and 3 (machine half) in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 2 — what landed.md` (sections 3, 7, 8 and 9). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the Simplify group's three values that cannot be typed, and Render Settings' device line drawing *last seen* as raw seconds (both on the board, ruled one small piece after the 38's piece 6); the rental pass and its watch (the board's START HERE item 3 carries every watch in the sitting's accounts). Left out because it is not a gap: the part banner is proved at the desk only, since no pass can cut the line in the middle of a submission to reach it.*

**A plan seam, for the next plan pass:**
- **A job sent with no scene named keeps no scene, so its settings load as unread answers.** `Render Settings` Y15's paragraph *Under which key the loading writes* (`Preparation/Architecture/Render Settings.md`) says the kept scene name arrives with the setup, so a scene is in hand and J3's pre-read set plays no part. A job submitted with no scene named has no kept scene. Built: its entries load into the pre-read set (J3), Y15a's condition kept, no scene ticked, nothing pinned. The build runner ruled it a plan gap. Tagged for that paragraph.

**Plan clauses met only in part:**
- **History does not draw the *Simplify was on* mark** (`Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md`, from `Preparation/Specs/Queue & History.md`'s *A job submitted with Simplify on says so on its entry*), though the kept setup has carried the answer since record version 25. A job rendered again with Simplify shows frames unlike the saved file and its entry does not say why.
- **`Rendering` A1a's second case is never met.** *The machine being browsed this run* has no run-held home on the desktop. So a resubmission whose server is gone goes by hole 3's ruling alone: one server connected opens its Files, several open the server list, none says so.
- **The pick bar's sentence may read false.** `Preparation/Design/File Browser.md` says picking a `.blend` gives it to whoever asked rather than opening Render Settings, and the bar says *It does not open the settings page.* The one caller is the resubmission's own page, so after the pick that page shows, with the file in it. If the sentence reads as false on the pass, it is Design's to reword.

**Where the record and the world stop agreeing:**
- **The pick and the part banner are held for the run.** Quit Smoozy with a pick in hand and the setup it held is gone. Quit with a part banner showing and the next run shows none: the unsent scenes' provisional records still resolve at the next connection (`State, identity and recovery` V1 step 2), but nothing names the scenes left unsent.
- **A carry-on's frame list and inventory can come from two attempts.** The frame list is the latest attempt record that has one; the inventory is the newest standing reading, and an attempt that kept the manifest's map writes no reading. Where the map moved between them, `Rendering` A4's figure is reckoned against the earlier map.
- **`Rendering` A10 follows a link.** A `.blend` that is a symbolic link whose target is gone reads as gone, and the pick bar opens.
- **The carried-on mark is written at submission**, from the page's memory of where it came from. Two Submits from one carried-on page make two jobs that both name the earlier one.
- **The band's moment is the earlier job's submission**, on this computer's clock, not when it rendered.

**What it costs.** A job with no scene named loses its scene-keyed answers' tick and pins on a resubmission. A Simplify render's entry says nothing of it. A quit loses a held pick or the list of unsent scenes. Nothing is rendered wrongly and nothing false is said, except possibly the pick bar's sentence.

**Due:** the Y15 seam at the runner's next plan pass; the *Simplify was on* mark beside the Simplify boxes' small piece, or else when the runner next maps what the plans promise and nothing builds; the pick bar's sentence on the next pass that presses *Submit again* on a moved file (the account's section 9); A1a's second case and the five places above only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### The 38's piece 3, machine half: no picture for a multi-layer job, and where the pictures and the record part (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 2 and 3 (machine half) in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 3, machine half — what landed.md` (sections 3, 5, 7 and 8). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the desktop half — fetching and showing the pictures, and `Queue & History` §7 step 4's fill-in of pictures a connection learns late — which the desktop half's builder reads from this account (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the 38, piece 3, desktop half, the picture.md`); a backlog when a job renders faster than one picture in about 2.4 seconds (`Queue & History` P1's own cost, §18 item 18); the rental pass on all four providers and its watches (the board).*

**Permanently unresolved under the Method, and what holds without it:**
- **A job whose only output is multi-layer OpenEXR gets no preview picture.** `Job files and outputs` B26 sends a multi-layer source to the image library bundled inside Blender's Python, and none of that library's names are on record, in the research or in any desk lookup. Under `Preparation/Build/Method.md` (the Creator's ruling of 2026-08-31: no further Blender research) it is permanently unresolved, not pending; the build runner ruled it so. **What holds without it:** the composition stops at the absence (`the_route_is_on_record`), each frame's preview record says *nothing to make*, and the render and its files are untouched. The job's entries have no picture; nothing false is said. From 5.0 a regular output path set to multi-layer is not told apart by the reading, so it is tried, loads with no pixels, and is recorded the same way. The account names what would close it: the library's own names and the multi-layer channel names (*Images* question 5), by a desk lookup like the property-name lookup of 2026-09-20.

**Where the pictures and the record part:**
- **A picture from a working format takes the job's view transform and nothing else of its colour treatment** (B25 names only the view transform). A job with a Look, exposure or gamma set gets a picture slightly unlike its own PNG. A file output node with its own colour override is copied in the scene's transform.
- **A frame published at one attempt is not made again when a later attempt renders it again** (P1a's set is *no published derivative*), so the picture stays the earlier render's.
- **A production cut by a supervisor restart leaves its record open.** The frame is queued again, so one frame can carry two records; reconciliation gives the old one up at the next start.
- **A picture can pass the 10 MiB byte cap.** It is written at the render's size up to B26a's target, 16 bits from a working format; the production records it and it is published anyway.
- **One journal event per picture published.** A very long job spends the journal's 32,768 entries faster, and a reader whose cursor falls behind loses older events (the shape of `Queue & History` §18 item 23). The pictures themselves are untouched.
- **The preview records accumulate**, one per frame, and are read once at each supervisor start.

**Not given a biting test:** removing a copy that did not finish from its staging name (seen only by its absence in the real-program test), and the ten-minute bound on one production's Blender.

**What it costs.** No picture for multi-layer-only jobs. A slightly different picture where a job sets more than its view transform. An old picture after a re-render. More journal and record use on long jobs. The renders and their files are never touched.

**Due:** the multi-layer gap stands as built unless the Creator rules otherwise; the cap, the journal, the accumulating records and the cut production if a pass meets one (the account's watch list); the two untested parts with the next piece that touches `the_preview_worker.rs`; the colour treatment and the later attempt's picture only if the Creator meets one.

**The multi-layer gap closed 2026-10-03 for a regular output path**, on the Creator's word (*"EXR is what people use all the time"*), by `Audits/Build/Step 10c/A picture for a multi-layer OpenEXR job — what landed.md` (committed at `9ef57530`). The library's names were read off the four Blenders at the desk and written into `Job files and outputs` B26 with sources entry 52a; the copy matches Blender's own PNG of the same frame in all four. **The entry's sentence that from 5.0 the reading does not tell a multi-layer regular output path apart was wrong**: it records `OPEN_EXR_MULTILAYER` in every version. A file a file output node gathers still has no picture, and is carried in the entry below. **The cap bullet's figure is corrected too**: the machine half's 440 KB for a 9000×3000 PNG's copy was measured on a 16-bit PNG by accident. From an eight-bit 9000×3000 PNG the single-layer route's copy is 12.9 MB, over the 10 MiB cap; it is published anyway, and a phone sent it would refuse it. The multi-layer route's copy of the same size is 208 KB. *Noted 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 3 (desktop half) and 6, the multi-layer picture, OptiX and the test copy's notes piece 1 in its place by the build runner.*

### The 38's piece 3, desktop half: a picture that is not there says nothing, and where the copies and the record part (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 3 (desktop half) and 6, the multi-layer picture, OptiX and the test copy's notes piece 1 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 3, desktop half — what landed.md` (sections 3, 4 and 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the stepper (owed, named on the board by the 0.1.8 map); a frame rendered again at a later attempt of the same job keeping the earlier picture (the machine half's entry above); the pass on all four providers and its watch (the board). Left out because it is not a gap: a frame published after the last connection a machine ever gets is never fetched, which is `Queue & History` P8 as written.*

**For the next plan pass — Design has no words:**
- **A preview picture cleared after thirty days, or one that never came, shows nothing and says nothing.** `Preparation/Specs/Queue & History.md` (*A job's frame preview*) promises the cleared case is said and leaves the wording to Design; `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md` has no sentence for it, so the builder invented none (its decision 8). The same blank stands for a picture never fetched and for one the machine could not make (since 2026-10-03, a multi-layer file a file output node gathers). The entry draws no picture column and no words. Tagged for `Preparation/Design/Queue & History.md`, the preview column.

**Where the copies and the record part:**
- **A copy whose file a removal could not delete** — at an entry's deletion, or a replaced copy — is no longer named by the record and stays on this computer. The age sweep removes only what the record names.
- **An entry removed inside an outer transaction that then rolls back** has lost its copies' files while its rows stand, so the entry draws a picture box the page cannot fill.
- **A preview fetched for a frame later rendered again by a carry-on** stays on the earlier job's entry and is never replaced there; the carry-on's own job has its own set.
- **The heading names the frame alone** where the event left the view layer's name out for the journal's budget; the machine's record is not read for it (P10a).
- **Events the cursor fell past are never fetched** (`Queue & History` §18 item 23). The fill-in that would recover them (§7 step 4, named in the machine half's account, section 8) is built on neither side.

**Not given a biting test:** the fetch thread per connection, the attribute read and the transfer over a live point, the command's bytes and the page's `blob:` address (each a few lines over tested seams; the account's watch covers them); and the preview class's name, read from `File Browser` O4's list and checked by nothing but its use.

**What it costs.** A cleared or missing picture leaves a silent blank where the spec promises a word. A failed removal leaves a small file on disk. A picture learned late is never shown. Nothing false is said about a render.

**Due:** the Design sentence at the runner's next plan pass; the fill-in when `Queue & History` §7 step 4 is next mapped, and before step 10c closes; the copies and the rollback only if a pass or the Creator meets one; the untested parts with the next piece that touches `src/the_previews.rs`.

### The 38's piece 6: which setup counts as retained, and what the card cannot tell (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 3 (desktop half) and 6, the multi-layer picture, OptiX and the test copy's notes piece 1 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 6 — what landed.md` (sections 3, 5, 7 and 9). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: the *Install it* offer (the map's piece 11); the pass on all four providers and its watch (the board). Closed by this piece and marked at their own entries: *The Set up card says No setup read from this server after every reopen*, and the *Last job rendered on* bullet of *Four things fix piece 2 left owed*.*

**A Build reading of the plan, for the next plan pass:**
- **"Retained until the next run of its kind" is read as the newest ready-action record by its last boundary** (`Rented-server runtime` §13 R1, as `Server Setup` Y6b narrows it), because nothing on the machine removes a record. Every older one stays in the store. A machine whose clock went backwards between two setups could name the older one; a recovery cut before its own first boundary ties with the run it took over, and the open one is named.

**A Design gap (Rule 10):**
- **A stopped setup last seen shows one line, not its steps.** `Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md`, *Cut off part-way*, asks for the steps greyed. The panel's own words say its steps were read just now and both its controls act on the machine, so the builder drew the card's one line instead (its decision 5). Design wants a sentence for the stopped panel while disconnected, or this reading confirmed.

**Where the card and the world part:**
- **A 4.5 job that rendered on the processor when asked for the cards may not be called a fault.** W4's *names the processor alone* is read only where the line closes `[CPU]`, and 4.5's processor line was not found transcribed. Permanently unresolved under the Method; what holds: the line is stated as printed and no fault is called.
- **A job whose payload asks nothing and whose recorded reading is gone** has an unread ask, so no fault is called from it.
- **A setup run by another copy of Smoozy after this one last read the machine** is not shown: this copy keeps reading the run it drove itself until it is relaunched.
- **Last seen is this computer's moment of reading**, held only for the run (hole 7). A server removed and added again in the same run shows the old server's reading until it is connected.
- **The Telegram line follows the role's desired state** (*enabled*), not whether the role worker is running.

**Not given a biting test:** the wiring inside `what_the_machine_said` (choosing `AReading` where no ready record stands, and holding each reading), which needs a running app; each part it calls is tested.

**What it costs.** Rarely, an older setup named, a newer one from another copy not shown, a GPU-asked job that rendered on a 4.5 processor not called out, or a Telegram line that says installed while its worker is down. A stopped setup, disconnected, hides its steps.

**Due:** the R1 reading and the stopped panel at the runner's next plan pass; the 4.5 line if a pass meets a 4.5 processor render (the account's watch 7), never by renting to answer it; the rest only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### A picture for a multi-layer OpenEXR job: the gathered file, the compositor, and what was not bitten (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 3 (desktop half) and 6, the multi-layer picture, OptiX and the test copy's notes piece 1 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/A picture for a multi-layer OpenEXR job — what landed.md` (sections 1.7, 5 and 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out because already carried elsewhere: a Look, exposure or gamma taking no part in the copy (the machine half's entry above, whose limit this route shares); the eight-bit 9000×3000 copy over the 10 MiB cap (the note under that entry); Linux never watched, and the rest of the account's section 8 (the board).*

- **A multi-layer file a file output node gathers still has no picture.** The reading records the node and not the entry the beauty reached, and choosing the only colour-shaped entry would be the by-position choice `Job files and outputs` B19a forbids. Each frame's record says *nothing to make … not known (B19)*. Closing it means the reading script recording that entry for a gathered node.
- **The copy is the view layer's own `Combined`, not the compositor's result.** A job whose compositor changes the picture (glare, a grade) gets a preview of the render before the compositor — what B19 asks for, and not what that job's PNG would show. Not tried.
- **A display other than the factory `sRGB`** is not followed: the copy takes the job's view transform with the factory display, as Blender's own single-layer route does. Not tried.
- **Not fully bitten:** naming Blender's own colour configuration bit only in 4.5, where the route fails without it. Whether 5.1 and 5.2, which set it in the process's surroundings, would also catch its loss was not reached.
- **Two files left outside the scratchpad**, because a command holding `rm` is refused: `/tmp/x` (a stray copy of the script body) and the real-reading desk test's own temporary folder, `$TMPDIR/smoozy-multi-layer-<pid>`, left by the bites C1 and C5. Both small and clearly that sitting's.

**What it costs.** A gathered multi-layer job has no picture; a composited job's picture is the render before the compositor. Nothing false is said and the render is untouched.

**Due:** the gathered file when the reading script is next opened for the beauty walk; the compositor and the display only if the Creator meets one; the bite with the next piece that touches `derivatives.rs`; the two files at the runner's next tidy.

### OptiX for NVIDIA cards: what the lookup cannot see (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 3 (desktop half) and 6, the multi-layer picture, OptiX and the test copy's notes piece 1 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/OptiX for NVIDIA cards — what landed.md` (section 7). Nothing else in this file carries this. Left out: the drawing's seven CUDA words, drawn since (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The drawing pass for the Creator's notes and OptiX — what was done.md`, committed at `89894aa4`); the sample Blender lines in tests that read `(CUDA)`, which are Blender's own words and stay; Debian, left alone by the plan's own `Server Setup` §13 item 27; the pass and its watch (the board).*

- **The lookup rests on `sh` being a program the system's loader starts.** It asks the loader what `sh` would load with the library preloaded. That holds on every Ubuntu and Debian image this project has read. On a system where it does not, nothing is listed, and a cards job is refused with *This server's NVIDIA driver has no OptiX library (libnvoptix.so.1), so it cannot render on the GPU.* on a machine that might have rendered. The lookup does not prove the driver works either; that stays `Rendering` L24 and L25's.

**What it costs.** On such a system, every cards job fails before the render with a sentence that blames the driver.

**Due:** the next pass's watch 2 (a cards job's line ends `--cycles-device OPTIX` and renders, on a machine whose `ldconfig -p` lists the library); after that, only if a pass or the Creator meets the refusal on a machine that has the library.

### The test copy's notes piece 2: where the dependency check's panel and the record part (2026-10-03)

*Added 2026-10-03 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the test copy's notes pieces 2, 3 and 4 and the drawing pass in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's notes on the test copy, piece 2 — what landed.md` (sections 4 and 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out: the pass and its watches (the board). The first point is the map's hole 3 as ruled; the map names it and does not carry it as owed.*

- **After a restart the panel does not know an earlier check the record holds.** Transfers' *Open the check* says *Nothing was read from the file…* with *Check again*, and *Check again* brings the answers back (`Dependency Upload & Remap` K4).
- **The *Reading the file…* mark is held for the run only.** A check reading when Smoozy quits leaves nothing behind; reopened, the panel says nothing was read.
- **The add box lists Settings' folders as they stood when the panel was last composed.** A folder changed in Settings while the box is open shows at the panel's next asking.
- **A press that joins a check already reading linked files changes nothing on screen**; its rows come when the running check ends.

**Not given a biting test** beyond reading its source: `the_check_is_begun`, which needs a running app. Every part it calls is tested.

**What it costs.** After a quit, a check already run has to be run again to be seen; a folder changed in Settings with the box open shows a moment late. Nothing false is said.

### The 38's piece 9: where a carried-on download, a leftover's *Remove* and *Which ones* fall short (2026-10-04)

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the test copy's notes pieces 5, 7 and 6, and 8, the 38's pieces 7, 9 and 15, and the two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 9 — what landed.md` (sections 3 and 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Left out: the fault that section found — a manual download's *cannot land* files told as *already in this folder* — fixed by the two transfer faults (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 — what landed.md`, fault 2); the two threads this piece closes, marked closed above; the pass and its watches (the board).*

- **The four values carry whole seconds.** A file rewritten in place to the same size inside the same second as its reading, keeping its inode, reads unchanged, and a resume carries on over it. `Server protocol and transfers` S13 accepted these four values; the pass-through digest proves only that what was read is what was written.
- **A leftover whose staging part a machine restart already removed**: *Remove* finds nothing, counts it gone and clears the record. The row goes and nothing is said; the map expected it to say so.
- **A leftover of an upload whose server row has a new installation identity** is outside *Remove*'s records, as it is outside the leftover row.
- **An auto-download's fetch cut by a dropped line still starts over**, as `Download` P3 has it (the account's decision 2), so R7's newer-version telling still never fires.
- ***Files on your computer* on an entry whose server row is gone** opens what the arrangement fetched but cannot test `Download` H5's second source, because the job's output folder is composed from the row's layout.
- **A name that is not valid text** cannot be matched by *Try again*, *Upload it again* or *Which ones*, because Transfers draws the landing path as text; *Try again* says the transfer is no longer held.
- **The *may be incomplete* line is composed by nothing** (`Download` E1 step 2's mark is the ending walk's verdict, and no record keeps it), so its *Which ones* is unreachable.
- **A failed upload that is neither refused nor changed** (a digest that disagreed, a file this computer could not read) is counted and drawn nowhere. No record tells its cause apart, so *Try again* on such a row is built and unreachable.
- **An arrangement switched on from the entry** reads its count as nothing until a pass reads the job's whole.

**What it costs.** A rare carry-on over a file changed within the same second; a leftover row that goes without a word; a few failed uploads with no row; a count that reads nothing for one pass. Nothing is lost on the server.

**Due:** the failed upload drawn nowhere and the *may be incomplete* line when the runner next maps what the plans promise and nothing builds, and before step 10c closes; the rest only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### The 38's piece 7: a linked file with no copy here, a stop the desk's Mac cannot make, and copies sent twice (2026-10-04)

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the test copy's notes pieces 5, 7 and 6, and 8, the 38's pieces 7, 9 and 15, and the two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 7 — what landed.md` (sections 3, 7 and 9), and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Two transfer faults after pieces 7 and 9 — what landed.md` (section 7, the check's own send). - **A linked `.blend` on the server with no copy on this computer** (`Dependency Upload & Remap` K5) gets no fixed copy: the fixing is derived only from a mirror this module placed. M13's *whichever route* is kept for every library the check finds on this computer; the K5 case is named, not built.
- **A fixed copy whose mirror the user then replaces through Files** is owed again (M16b) and made again at the next check. Until then the render reads the fixing made from the old version, and the band's disturbance mark (V13a) says the file needs checking.

- **The check's own send starts over silently.** It abandons the part and arranges afresh, so no row says *from the start*, where Files' own upload now does (the two transfer faults, fault 1).

**What it costs.** A linked file kept only on the server is not fixed; one render may read a fixing made from an old version; a redundant copy; a start-over not said. Nothing false is said about the files.

### The 38's piece 11: what a connection's telling leaves (2026-10-04)

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 8 and 11 and the setup's final check and Install Telegram in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 11 — what landed.md` (sections 3, 5 and 7). Nothing else in this file carries these. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` K2, L2 and L3, and `Preparation/Design/Settings.md`. Left out: the lock (its own entry below); *Install it* not wired (closed the same day by `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The setup's final check and Install Telegram — what landed.md`); the watch (the board).*

- **Settings' *not reached* list and the card disagree about a recycled machine.** Where a recycle gives a new installation, the old told entries stop counting: a connection sends nothing and the card offers *Install it*. **But Settings' list counts entries under any installation** (`App/Desktop app and delivery/src/surface/telegram.rs`, its *fix-here item 10*), so it still names that server, and no connection will clear it. Only pressing *Install it* does.
- **A new token reaches the token file, and a role worker already running keeps the old one.** It reads the token at its start (`Telegram` K2), and *enabled* is not sent again where it is already told, so nothing restarts it. Whoever builds the role worker decides whether it reads the token again or the delivery restarts it. **Until then, a machine's next message from the new bot cannot be watched.**
- **A change in Settings while a server stays connected** reaches it only at its next connection (the builder's decision 5). Settings names it as not reached meanwhile, which is true.
- **A file placed whose told entry the record refused** is sent again at the next connection, on the same bytes.
- **Servers set up with Telegram on before this piece** were listed as not reached until their first connection after it, which sends *enabled* once more and acts on nothing.
- **Not given a biting test:** the order inside the delivery (files before the switch), which the desk cannot see; and the delivery's thread at connection, which needs a running app (each part it calls is tested).

**What it costs.** A recycled server named in Settings until *Install it* is pressed. A replaced bot token that the phone does not use until the role worker restarts, once that worker exists.

**Due:** the token with the piece that builds the Telegram role worker, beside *The phone's stop reaches nothing*; Settings' rule at the runner's next plan pass over `Telegram` L3; the rest only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

### The lock never reaches a machine: no request, store or reader (2026-10-04)

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 8 and 11 and the setup's final check and Install Telegram in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The 38, piece 11 — what landed.md` (section 1, stop 1, and section 7), with the build runner's ruling of 2026-10-04 and the Creator's decision of the same day. Nothing else in this file carried it. Tagged for `Preparation/Architecture/Telegram.md` L1, L5 and L6, `Access` M1 to M3, `Security and licence` §11 (K1 to K4), and `Rented-server runtime`'s Telegram role worker.*

The 38's piece 11 delivers everything a server was not told on each connection **except the lock**. **The machine has nowhere to take it**: no request carries a lock policy, the machine program has no store for one, and the role worker's reader (`Telegram`'s `ReadsTheLockPolicy`) has only its stand-in. What the machine would need:
1. one command request, *the lock policy is delivered*, carrying `Access` M1's whole published value, recorded before it acts (`Server protocol and transfers` C4);
2. a machine-level store for it, written durably before the answer (`Security and licence` K3; `State, identity and recovery` §1.2);
3. the role worker's real `ReadsTheLockPolicy` over that store (`Telegram` L5).

After that, the desktop half is one more arm in `what_this_server_is_owed`, one request and one acknowledgement. Meanwhile `Access`'s *which servers the lock has not reached* names every server Smoozy has told nothing of it, which is all of them.

**Ruled by the build runner, 2026-10-04: a later machine piece.** **And very likely dropped.** The Creator decided on 2026-10-04 that Smoozy becomes free and open source (`Audits/Build/Free and open source/The Creator's decision, 2026-10-04.md`), and that decision names *the lock reaching machines* among the paid layer that goes. The map of that change (`Audits/Build/Free and open source/The map — what free and open source changes, and in what order.md`, section 4) lists this lock half of piece 11 as dropped and says this entry closes.

**What it costs.** Nothing a user meets today: the role worker that would read the lock is not built either.

**Due:** closes with the free-and-open-source plan pass that withdraws `Telegram` L5 and L6 and `Security and licence` §11. Only if the paid layer were kept after all, with the later machine piece the runner ruled.


Superseded by the Creator’s decision of 2026-10-04; recorded 2026-10-26. Paid-layer work is withdrawn. Earlier rulings and closures remain as history.

### 0.1.8 as released ends a setup with Telegram on and no Blender at its final check (2026-10-04)

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after pieces 6b, 6c and 6d.md`, given the 38's pieces 8 and 11 and the setup's final check and Install Telegram in its place by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The setup's final check and Install Telegram — what landed.md` (sections 1, 5 and 7). Nothing else in this file or on the board carries it. Tagged for the next release.*

The machine's final check decided whether a run had published a Blender from how far the run had got, and the Telegram step comes after the Blender step. So **every setup with Telegram on and no Blender asked for ended failed at *Run Smoozy's final check***, including *Set up again* on a server whose Blender was installed separately, and *Install it* could not run at all. **Fixed in the tree and committed at `5c0a9010`**: the check now reads the run's step list and its marker together. **Not released**: 0.1.8's machine program still has the fault, so a pass that watches either run must place the desk-built program, never 0.1.8.

**Not given a biting test:** the press of *Install it* in a running app (the arm is read from the source; the run is tested against the real program).

**What it costs.** On 0.1.8, a user with Telegram on who sets up without picking a Blender sees the setup fail at its last step.

**Due:** at the next release, which carries the fix; the watch is the board's.

*Added 2026-10-04 by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after the second notes' first pieces.md`, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's second notes, piece 1 — what landed.md` (section 7), `… piece 1b — what landed.md` (section 6) and `… piece 2 — what landed.md` (section 6). - **A job submitted before piece 1 keeps its recorded map on a re-run** (R13), so its folders stay one level deeper (`Render/PNG`). Its queue box can still say PNG 4 where 2 are written. Only the machine's own inventory could say otherwise, and the piece did not reach for it.

Both apply the one rule, so they cannot disagree on which layers render. One home on `Payload` in `Render Settings — machine` would make it one, at the cost of a machine-program change.

**What it costs.** An old or adopted job's queue box promising more files than the job writes. Two copies of a five-line turn that could drift in how they copy a name.

Something in that run — the reading, the version ask, or the launched-and-undone render — made the file's own folder.

**What it costs.** A page that names a place the job will not use, for a Mac path or a too-long one. A job sent to a server not yet updated failing at its start. A full disk where the file sits. Empty folders left behind.

**Due:** the older-server case at the next release, which servers follow; the rest only if a pass or the Creator meets one.

Piece 4's desk ran the desktop crate and ten other crates there, and compiled the whole workspace's tests without running them. Pieces 2b and 5 each ran the desktop crate there before the later pieces landed. **No run has tested the whole workspace on the tree as committed at `22f13b15`.**

**What it costs.** A fault between the four pieces would be found by a later piece's desk, not by these.

**Due:** at the start of the next sitting, before the queue half or piece 6b is built.

The board was the only place it was written, so this is its written home.*

And I'm thinking that pre-existing buttons is the way to do it. Needs careful planning." Asked again in the fourteenth notes, note 6. They are in Blender's own output because Smoozy's placed script prints them there and Smoozy reads them back. Nothing promises a filtered log. **The want**: leave lines that begin with Smoozy's own markers out at the moment of showing or sending, never out of the kept log. **Due:** when the Creator asks, or the Specs' words sweep reaches the log.

### Reading a file in Render Settings also checks its dependencies, and the reverse (2026-10-11)

*Creator-stated, 2026-10-11, the ninth notes on the test copy, note 4 (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's tests of the test copy/Ninth notes, 2026-10-11.md`). **Planned on 2026-10-12 and withdrawn on 2026-10-13, before anything was built**, when the Creator deferred it: *"Let's just defer it for later."* A want under Rule 7. Parked by the plan writer holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the ninth notes, note 4 deferred, the plans put back.md` (Claude Opus 5.5).*

The Creator opened the dependency check after reading a file in Render Settings, and it said nothing was read from the file. In their words: *"reading a file should give Smoozy information about everything there is in the file, including all of its dependencies. So the dependency checker should already know what's up with the file when it is read from render settings. And vice versa."* **The plans say the opposite on purpose**: nothing runs the check for the user, not even reading the file's settings (`Specs/Dependency Upload & Remap`, *Checking a file*, Creator-stated 2026-07-28). **The want**: either read starts the other, on the same file with the same Blender, each only where none for that file is running or already done; they stay two separate reads, each landing in its own panel.

**Where the planned text is**: commit `ec71463d` wrote it into `Specs/Dependency Upload & Remap` (*Connections*, *Checking a file*, line 297), `Specs/Render Settings` (*Pre-load Settings*, *Pre-load is not a dependency check*), `Specs/Whole app` walkthrough step 11, `Architecture/Dependency Upload & Remap` G1b (kept there, struck) and `Architecture/Render Settings` Q4. The map's design is under *Ninth note 4* in `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Creator's eighth notes 1 to 4 and ninth notes — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, with its build piece N4. **What it costs**, by the map: one more Blender start each time either is pressed (about 3 seconds), plus the check's search of the user's folders; one Blender opening for both would need the two reading scripts merged.

**Due:** when the Creator asks for it. It enters at Specs (`Dependency Upload & Remap`, `Render Settings`, `Whole app`), reversing the rule of 2026-07-28 on their word.

Once Delete is built, Files can delete Smoozy's own things on a server like any other folder — its program area, the job folders, an installed Blender. Nothing warns, and the plans say nothing should. **The want**: a warning before such a delete, saying what it is and what stops working.

### Taking up the held jobs an earlier setup of a server left behind (2026-10-01)

*Added 2026-10-01 by the builder of step 10c's thirteenth-pass fix piece 2, on the build runner's ruling (A2-2 not written). A want under Rule 7: the plan never offered it, and the card now tells the truth instead (`Preparation/Design/Server Setup.md` § When work on the server holds the setup back).*

A server removed with *Leave it running* while first-frame checks wait, then added again, cannot be set up: the held jobs block the program's replacement (`Rented-server runtime` G21 to G23), and nothing in Smoozy can answer them. **The proof at the desk found three reasons, and any way out needs all three answered** (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Thirteenth-pass fix piece 2 — what landed.md`): the removal keeps their local records under the removed row, so `Queue & History` A5's offer never names them and the new row's Queue never draws them; *Track it* is not built; and `State, identity and recovery` Y9 forbids a new installation adopting another's state. The holds themselves do survive a commit, and the machine takes an answer from any connection. **The want**: a way for the person to answer or drop those jobs from Smoozy, so the machine can be set up again without renting a new one. `Rented-server runtime`'s residual *A hold blocking a package commit indefinitely* says *"the user meets nothing"*, which stops being true here; it is owed a correction with whatever is decided. **Corrected 2026-10-01 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (thirteenth-pass fix piece 4), on the build runner's ruling**: the residual now says the user meets the setup card's count of held jobs, and points here for the way out.

**Due:** after 0.1.7 is released, weighed with the other wants. It enters at Specs (`Queue & History`, `Server Setup`), and reaches Architecture (`State, identity and recovery` Y9, `Queue & History` A5) behind it.

### Saying a render ran out of memory, not only *status -9* (2026-09-30)

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/The fixes after the eleventh pass — what each piece is, which files it owns, and in what order.md`, fault 7. A want under Rule 7; nothing promised is broken.*

On the eleventh pass the 5.2 battle scene took Blender to 20.5 GB and the system ended it on a 21 GB machine. The job was recorded *failed* and said only *status -9*. **That is true, and the plan chose it**: `Specs/Rendering` says Smoozy usually cannot say why a job failed, and Design names a full disk as the one cause Smoozy reads. **The want**: say the render ran out of the machine's memory where that is what happened.

**Due:** after 0.1.7 is released, weighed with the other wants. It enters at Specs (`Rendering`).

### Removing the empty `.smoozy-staging` folders an upload leaves (2026-09-30)

*Added 2026-09-30 by the paperwork piece of step 10c (eleventh-pass fix piece 11), from the same map, fault 13. A want under Rule 7; nothing promised is broken.*

A folder upload leaves an empty `.smoozy-staging` in every folder it wrote to. `File Browser` X12 shows the staging folder as reserved on purpose, and no clause removes an empty one. **The Creator will meet them on the Vast pass**, in every folder of a huge upload. **The want**: remove an empty staging folder at the request's end — per the map, one sentence under `Server protocol and transfers` S1 and a small change at a request's end.

**Met on the Vast pass, 2026-10-01**: a `.smoozy-staging` folder was left in every folder that took an upload (`/`, `/pv-huge`, `/pv-huge/unpacked-5.2/textures`, `/pv-huge/blend`), and each is listed in Files (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/The Vast pass — the Creator's real-world tests.md`, *Smaller, as seen*). Noted by the the paperwork piece of step 10c after the Vast pass.

**Most of this is settled by the *Show hidden files* checkbox, 2026-10-02, and the item stays open.** With the box off — the default — the staging folders are hidden like any other name starting with a period (`Preparation/Architecture/File Browser.md` X12 as amended, and L21), so the user no longer meets them in Files. **What is left**: the empty folders are still on the server, and a user who ticks the box still sees one in every folder an upload wrote to. Weigh whether removing them is still worth doing.

**Due:** if the Creator asks for it after the Vast pass; otherwise after 0.1.7 is released. It enters at Architecture (`Server protocol and transfers` S1).

**Met again on the check after 0.1.8, 2026-10-02, and ruled by the build runner not a fault against the plans** (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/After 0.1.8, the checks' leftovers — what landed.md`, item 5): an empty `.smoozy-staging` is still left in every folder an upload writes into. `Server protocol and transfers` S1 puts staging in a Smoozy-named folder beside the final name and S28 removes abandoned staged material by record; nothing says the empty folder goes. Both download routes step over it, and since that piece so does a folder the check sends. 0.1.7 is released, so the Due above has fired: weigh it with the other wants. Added by the records writer (Claude Opus 5.5) holding `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Brief — the paperwork after the checks' leftovers.md`.

### Saying *the path is too long*, and naming the file, when one local path stops an upload (2026-09-29)

*Added 2026-09-29 by the paperwork piece, from the transfer map's huge-files list and `Audits/Build/Step 10c/Huge files and huge folders at the desk — what was found.md`. A want under Rule 7; nothing promised is broken.*

One local path too deep to read stops a whole 10,000-file upload before it starts, as `File Browser` U1 requires (no request is written from part of an enumeration), with *Smoozy could not read this on your computer, so the upload did not start:* and the whole path. The sentence is true. **The want**: say that the path is too long, and name the file rather than print the whole path.
