Superseded by the Creator’s decision of 2026-10-04; recorded 2026-10-26. Paid-layer work is withdrawn. Earlier rulings and closures remain as history.

### Telling the user, before they disconnect a repairing server, what goes with the period

**Parked from the Architecture stage's scenario replays, 2026-08-23.** Item R30 in `Audits/Architecture/Scenario replays/Consolidated.md` found that ending a connected period by hand while a repair is running silently drops the last-known queue reading and every live reading, and moves the server out of the removal warning's third row into its last. The implementer repaired the half that was Architecture's — `Preparation/Architecture/Servers.md` **S11a** now states the loss plainly — and **declined the other half and said why**, which is what brings it here.

**The moment.** A server's connection has broken and Smoozy is repairing it. The user, seeing *Reconnecting…*, disconnects it by hand. That ends the period, and with the period goes the last queue reading Smoozy held — so the removal warning that would have told them what is running on that machine now has nothing to read, and says less than it would have said a second earlier.

**Why it is a want and not a defect.** Nothing is wrong and no promise breaks. `Preparation/Specs/Servers.md` calls this *"the ordinary disconnect"* and says *"No separate action is needed for it."* Saying anything before the act would put something in front of the user that no spec grants — a warning on an action the spec deliberately made plain — which is the third of `Method`'s Creator moments and not an implementer's to write. It is recorded as a declined check in `Servers` §17.

**What it would take.** A spec sentence saying the disconnect on a repairing server costs the last-known reading, and where the user is told. That is a change to what the user gets and is the Creator's alone.

  **Due:** the Creator's, whenever they want it. **It blocks nothing** — the architecture is written on the current spec, the loss is stated where a builder will meet it, and taking this up would add a telling rather than move a mechanism.

### A reference the check could not pair produces no line anywhere, and Blender's own missing-file complaint is not observed

**Parked from the Architecture stage, 2026-09-02, by the implementer holding `Dependency Upload & Remap`. It is a want and not a fault**, and it is filed here rather than answered there.

**The moment.** The check found a reference and could not resolve it — the file was not found, or it was found and could not be sent — so no pair is composed for it. `Dependency Upload & Remap` **M8** leaves such a reference *"exactly as recorded"*, and the marker that document writes reports **pairs only**: applied, or not applied with the value found. So the reference that certainly will not resolve produces no line at all, while **M9d**, written the same day, gives every applied pair a field saying whether assignment alone is established for its kind — a statement about a case that may well have worked. **The certain case is the quieter of the two.**

**What the want asks for.** That Smoozy observe **Blender's own missing-file complaint** as the bytes pass the worker's copy loop, and state on the job's entry that the render ran with something it could not find. Blender says it out loud — the library case's own observed lines are in that document at M8, `Warning: Unable to open '…': No such file or directory` and the two beside it.

**The precedent, and it is exact.** `Queue & History` **E13a**, written 2026-09-02 out of the colour-management ruling, does this for a colour space the machine does not hold: observed in passing at the worker's own copy loop on E13's discipline, both streams watched, no new channel, no reading of state, the render's ending untouched, and a statement on the entry the user already opens. **The same shape would serve here**, and it is what makes this worth raising rather than noting.

**Why it is a want and not a hole.** The user was already told, at submission: the missing-assets warning stands over exactly these references, with V12's counts, and they chose to go on. And **Blender's own words are kept verbatim and are reachable** — `Queue & History` E7 keeps every byte of both streams and E9 carries them into history — so the complaint is in the record the entry already shows. What is missing is a statement composed over words the user can already read, which is an improvement rather than a silence.

**What it would cost.** A statement on the entry that no spec grants, and a per-version reading of line shapes nobody has written down — E13a's own residue at `Queue & History` §18 item 27 is the same shape of cost, and there will be no further Blender research to reduce it.

  **Due:** the Creator's, whenever they want to weigh it. **It blocks nothing** — no document owes it anything, no clause was changed for it, and `Dependency Upload & Remap` §13 records that it was raised and left here.

### Two things about pinned libraries that nobody has read, and one of them is the whole replay defence

**Parked from the Build stage, 2026-09-07, by `Access` Round 1's Verdict and filed here by that round's close check, which found they had never been filed.** They are questions to be answered by reading a publication, not by a machine, not by a rental and not by anybody's recollection.

**Headcount is worth nothing on either.** Several models doubting the same thing about the same library is one training set speaking twice, and the Verdict says so in terms. **Do not write a remembered answer for either of these.**

**The first: what the pinned signature library guarantees about 64 raw bytes.** `Access` A4 fixes the rule — two 32-byte unsigned halves, most significant byte first, in that order, and never the tagged encoding — and the code hands all sixty-four to the library's own constructor. **Whether that constructor reads the halves in that order, and whether it also accepts the mirror-image form of the same signature, is stated nowhere in this project.**

**What breaks if it comes back wrong:** every genuine response becomes *could not be reached*, and A4's own paragraph says what follows — the count locks a paying copy at seven days, with nothing on the screen and nothing the user can do to clear it. **Nothing is forgeable either way**, which is why this is the lesser of the two.

**What bounds it today and must not be removed:** the exact version pin, and the fixed cross-runtime vector test, which would catch a reversal.

**The second, and it is the more serious by a wide margin: whether the pinned randomness library reads the operating system's cryptographic random source on all three systems.** The whole of the replay defence is thirty-two fresh bytes from that source and nothing else. **`Access` D5 already cites `Queue & History` Y1 for exactly that claim about exactly that source — and this call is not written against that citation and carries no label at all.**

**What breaks if it comes back wrong:** anybody who can see or predict a request can replay an old *entitled* response and hold a lock open indefinitely.

**How each is answered.** The first by reading that library's own documentation at the version pinned. The second the same way, **or by pointing the call at Y1's citation if that citation already covers it** — which costs nothing and should be established first.

  **Due:** before the licence service is deployed, and before nothing earlier. **It blocks nothing today** — the service answers nobody, no envelope has ever crossed a wire, and both pins are exact. **What it must not do is arrive as a surprise on the day the first paying user is locked out.**

### What the nineteenth notes and the words sweep left, 2026-10-27/28

*Added 2026-10-28 by the build runner, from the accounts under `Audits/Build/Step 10c/` (note 2, note 7, the video wants) and `Audits/Build/Step 13/` (the four words accounts). The small code-side leftovers — words that needed a code change, three places where the drawing and the code disagree, two sentences that say the wrong thing, note 7's two missing lines, and the Telegram video limit re-read — are briefed under `Audits/Build/Step 13/` and are not repeated here.*

- **Taking one act out of the setup line is not built** (note 2; Architecture `Server Setup` Y9a item 6). Only *Cancel setup*, which stops the run in hand and everything behind it. A want; it enters at Design.
- **A driver swap does not drive the setup line** (note 2; Y9a item 8): what was pressed during a swap is named, not begun, even when the swap ended without a restart. `src/servers/the_driver_swap.rs`.
- **How a phone shows a video that is not MP4, sent with `sendVideo`** (the video wants; Architecture `Telegram` §21 item 35, unresolved). Needs a live Telegram pass with a non-MP4 job.
- **Code names that no longer match their words** after the sweep (`A_CONNECTION_IS_NOT_BUILT`, `NO_FRAMES_HAVE_BEEN_SAVED`, `NOTHING_WAS_MOVED_IT_IS_HERE` and others; final sweep account, *Skipped*). Nothing the user sees.
- **Every change since 2026-10-27 is unseen by any auditor**, like everything since the audits were paused until the Creator has tested everything.

**What it costs.** One want undone, one rare case named instead of carried out, one unknown on the phone, and names that mislead a reader of the code.

**Due:** the first two at the next Design sitting on `Server Setup`; the third with the next live Telegram pass; the fourth whoever next opens those files; the audits when the Creator calls them.

### A blank white window at start, cause unknown

*Added 2026-10-29 by the build runner, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/A rented pass for the twentieth notes.md` (*Other observations*) and `Two faults from the twentieth notes' pass — what landed.md` (item 2).*

The pass's throwaway copy opened to a white window, twice, and Command-R loaded the page. Reading the code found no cause and nothing that makes it development-only; one possible timing question is named in that account's sources file. Nothing was changed, on purpose: a reload or a delay without knowing which failure happened would hide it.

**What it costs.** A user may see a blank Smoozy until they reload, and nothing tells them to.

**Due:** at the next rented or desk pass that runs a throwaway copy — capture the page's own load and its first replies from the program when the window is white — and before 0.2.0 is published.

*Later, 2026-10-29, from `Audits/Build/Step 10c/A Telegram pass for the twentieth notes.md`:* it happened again, launched from the shell; **the page had loaded completely** (`tauri://localhost`, ready, 97 ms, no console error) and appeared as soon as the copy was brought to the front — no reload. That points at the window not being drawn until it is activated, perhaps only when launched in the background from a shell; it is not yet shown either way for a copy opened from Finder.

### Windows signing through SignPath: what stays unknown until tried (2026-11-01)

*Added 2026-11-01 by the plan pass for Want 1 of the twenty-sixth notes (`Audits/Build/Step 10c/Windows signing through the free open-source route, plan pass — what was done.md`); `Desktop app and delivery` E7a and E8a.*

- **Whether SignPath accepts Smoozy.** Acceptance is its own call, it asks reputation of a downloadable program, and two parts may need explaining: the root-certificate list's non-OSI licence and the update check (E7a).
- **Which authority issues SignPath Foundation's certificate, and whether it is in Microsoft's Trusted Root Program** — which is what Smart App Control admits. Read off the first signed file's chain at the desk.
- **Whether the signature carries a timestamp**, so an installed copy's signature stays valid after the certificate expires. SignPath's pages do not say. Read off the first signed file.
- **Whether a Windows 11 desktop with Smart App Control on runs the signed file, and what SmartScreen shows on the first download and on an update Smoozy placed itself** (Z17, item 6). No desktop Windows is reachable, so this is permanently unresolved on this project's hardware.
- **Whether a revoked certificate stops copies already installed from starting.** No page read says.

**What it costs.** Until the first closes, Windows goes out unsigned, with E8a's possible block; until the second, whether signing lifts that block is not known; until the third, whether an installed copy still counts as signed once the certificate expires is not known. The fourth means no promise of a quiet first start can be made. The fifth matters only if SignPath ever revokes.

**Due:** the first when SignPath answers the Creator's application; the second and third at the first release SignPath signs; the fourth only if a desktop Windows 11 computer is ever at hand, or a user reports it; the fifth only if SignPath ever pauses or revokes.

### Windows and Linux notifications: what reading the code cannot settle (2026-11-01)

*Added 2026-11-01 by Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code subagent), the builder holding `Audits/Build/Step 11/Brief — Windows and Linux notifications read for the Mac's faults.md`; the account is `Audits/Build/Step 11/Windows and Linux notifications, read for the Mac's faults — what landed.md`. `Desktop app and delivery` T28 and T30, items 13, 51 and 68.*

- **On Windows a notification is a popup alone.** No component server is registered, and Microsoft says a toast without one is not kept in the notification centre. So a user away from the screen for the few seconds it shows never sees it, and nothing is left to click later. Registering one per user, unelevated, with its identifier on the Start-menu shortcut, is what Microsoft documents; it is unobservable here and so it is a want.
- **A click on a Windows popup after Smoozy has quit, or on a Linux notification after Smoozy has quit, does nothing that is documented.** T30 promises nothing there.
- **On Linux, bringing the window forward may be refused.** The specification can send a token for that just before the click (`ActivationToken`) and Smoozy does not read it, so a desktop that guards focus may leave Smoozy behind, though the page still goes to the job or the server.
- **Whether a Linux desktop shows the picture sent by its file address**, or files Smoozy's notifications under the `Smoozy.desktop` entry, is unobserved.
- **A throwaway copy on Windows would take the real Start-menu shortcut.** A development build started with `--throwaway-copy=` makes or points the user's own `Smoozy.lnk` at itself (E3's sentence of 2026-11-01) and posts under the release's identifier, so the Start menu would then open the throwaway program. Nobody runs one on Windows; the Mac's test copy has its own identity.

**What it costs.** On Windows, the notification reaches only a user who sees the popup; on Linux, the window may stay behind its page; the last costs nothing until somebody runs a Windows throwaway copy.

**Due:** the first only if a Windows user reports missing notifications, or a desktop Windows is ever at hand; the second and fourth only if a desktop of either system is ever at hand (permanently unresolved on this project's hardware, items 2 and 3); the third only if a Linux user reports it; the last before any agent runs a throwaway copy on Windows.
